**Owner:** Evolith Architecture Board
**Last Updated:** 2026-08-08 (**One gap closed by doing the irreversible thing the board had deliberately deferred — and the measurement taken first is what makes it worth recording.** `GT-622` → DONE, and the irreversible half the board had deferred is what closed it: the 210 orphaned `.github/workflows/ci.yml:codeql` code-scanning analyses are deleted from `refs/heads/main` (201) and `refs/heads/develop` (9), the dead key is absent from both, and the alert corpus is byte-identical across the deletion — 242 open / 82 dismissed / 60 fixed, CodeQL 75 / Scorecard 158 / Trivy 9. **Three of that row’s own claims did not survive re-measurement, and the row had already been re-measured twice without any of them surfacing, because each pass checked the COUNT and none checked the CLAIM:** the check was `neutral`, not red, from PR #250 onward; the configuration the warning names is on `refs/heads/develop`, not `main`, so only PRs into `develop` still carried it while `main` came back clean on its own from PR #420 with all 201 orphans still in place; and the 9 analyses on `develop` — the ones that mattered — were never counted. **The field that turned an owner judgement call into an easy one had never been read:** all 210 analyses carry `results_count: 0`, so what was discarded is 210 records of “scanned, found nothing” from a configuration dead since June. The irreversibility was real; the loss was not. 22 analyses under the dead key are deliberately left on `refs/pull/{4..17}/merge`, ephemeral per-PR refs that can never be the base of a PR. **Also observed while measuring, and not part of this closure:** the required-context set on `main` and `develop` is now **8**, having gained `Secret Detection (gitleaks)` — the promotion `GT-653` recorded as its one remaining item. The closure criterion that could only be observed on a PR into `develop` was observed on PR #440, the PR carrying this very closure, 105s after it opened — written after reading the check, not before. Counters recomputed from the rows: **640 / 653 done · 3 in progress · 3 pending · 7 deferred**.)
**Last Updated (wave):** 2026-08-14 (**Six gaps registered from a competitive benchmark, and what was NOT registered is the part worth reading.** An owner-requested benchmark of The Agile Monkeys' Facility (`sdlc.theagilemonkeys.com`, `github.com/theam/Facility`) produced eight candidate rows; **two did not survive measurement against this repository and are deliberately absent** — "no calibration instrument" (refuted: `evolith calibrate report` ships a confusion matrix, false-block rate, Wilson interval and κ, [`GT-585`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-585) criterion 2) and "no observability connector" (refuted: `LangfuseEvidenceAdapter` exists and is exported, so the row was narrowed to the absent CONSUMER, [`GT-674`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-674)). A third was narrowed the same way: "nothing re-verifies the published artifact" is false as written — one check exists, `evolith-cli --help` at release time — so [`GT-671`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-671) is scoped to what that check cannot see. **The registered six are `GT-669`…`GT-674`**, three P1 and three P2, every absence claimed in them read out of this repository's own code before registration and quoted with file and line. **Ids taken by union of branches**, not from the local maximum: `GT-668` is the highest on every remote ref, so allocation starts at `GT-669`. Counters recomputed from the rows: **654 / 672 done · 2 in progress · 6 pending · 10 deferred**.)
**Gap Details:** [Gap Reference Catalog](./gap-reference-catalog.md)

This board is the single source of truth for technical debt, gaps, opportunities, enablers, priority, and status. Select a gap ID to open its problem statement, purpose, evidence, closure criteria, and references.

**Live In-Flight Claims:** Before starting or taking over a gap, run `node .harness/scripts/ci/50-validate-gap-claim.mjs --claims-markdown`. It reads open pull requests and their diffs live, then prints each claimed gap with its pull request, branch, source (`declared`, `diff`, or both), and claim status. The table is intentionally generated at read time rather than committed here, so it cannot go stale when a new PR opens.

> One table with every gap and tracked activity. `GT-*` IDs link to their full detail in the catalog; `MT-A*` IDs link to the supporting Multi-Topology implementation plan, but this table remains the canonical status source. Order: pending first (by criticality P0→P3, then complexity XS→XL), then completed (same criteria). GitHub renders Markdown statically (no interactive sorting or search): the **Component** column categorizes and GitHub file search (`/`) finds an ID or term.

| ID | Gap | Component | Phase | Criticality | Complexity | Status |

| [`GT-671`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-671) | **Nothing re-verifies the PUBLISHED artifact after the day it is published, and the one post-publish check is `--help`.** `sdk-cli-release.yml` is the only workflow that installs from the registry — `npm install -g @beyondnet/evolith-cli@<version>` followed by `evolith-cli --help` (`:328-332`) — while the functional smoke immediately above it runs `init` against the packaged BINARY downloaded from build artifacts, not against the npm install (`:317-325`). Both run once, inside the release. **Exactly two workflows in the repository carry a `schedule:`** — `opa-parity.yml` and `openssf-scorecard.yml` — so nothing periodic touches the published artifact at all. **The class has already cost twice:** [`GT-634`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-634) is a defect of the published CLI that appeared AFTER publication (a `^1.1.0` range that cannot cross a major, resolving `latest` onto a pre-wave SDK) and was found by hand while doing something else; and [`GT-625`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-625)'s own closure record ticks its criterion against "a locally packed tarball, not against the registry". Every check in this repository measures the tree; what customers touch is the tarball. Registered 2026-08-14 from an owner-requested benchmark of The Agile Monkeys' Facility, whose weekly synthetic canary exists for exactly this reason. The closing criterion is that the canary be OBSERVED red against a version known to be broken (`cli@1.2.0`), not argued to be. | `Infra` | Cross | P1 | S | `PENDING` |
| [`GT-670`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-670) | **The one organic adjudication the product already captures — an approved waiver — is never turned into the calibration label that instrument is waiting for.** `evolith waiver` records the `correlationId` of the verdict being waived (`waiver.command.ts:229`) and the domain `Waiver` carries the `fingerprint` of the suppressed violation plus `reason`, `requestedBy`, `approvedBy`, `approvedAt` and a hard `expiresAt` (`domain/waiver.ts:29-48`), persisted by `FileWaiverStore`. **An APPROVED waiver is a human deciding that a blocking violation should not have blocked** — exactly `humanBlocked: false` for that rule on that subject, which is the label `evolith calibrate report` consumes (`{ subject, rulesetId, gateBlocked, humanBlocked }`, `calibrate.command.ts:56`). Nothing connects them: `grep -rn "waiver" src/sdk/cli/src/commands/calibrate/` returns no matches. [`GT-585`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-585) is DEFERRED on "no organic label corpus until something runs in production"; **this row is the missing channel, not a second instrument** — the waiver ledger IS that corpus and nothing reads it. Registered 2026-08-14 from the Facility benchmark, whose watchtower joins each run receipt to its eventual outcome. The first formulation ("nothing measures whether a gate was right") was REFUTED by the existing calibrate command and narrowed to this. **The subtle criterion is the confirming label:** a rejected waiver must export as `humanBlocked: true`, or the corpus contains only disagreement and every rate reads 100 %. | `Governance` | Cross | P1 | M | `PENDING` |
| [`GT-669`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-669) | **A recurring finding has no path to a tenant-authored deterministic rule, so the ratchet is our private practice and not the product's capability.** This repository ratchets its own findings into guards — 112 files under `.harness/scripts/ci/`, each traceable to the board row that produced it — and none of that loop is reachable by a tenant. The only rule-facing CLI surface is `evolith rulesets`, whose own description is "List the ruleset packs this Core can evaluate" (`rulesets.command.ts:39-40`): read-only, 138 lines, no author, validate or publish path. The corpus lives in `src/rulesets/**` inside the Core repository and `evolith upgrade` copies it INTO the satellite, so the only way a tenant adds a rule today is to edit a file the next upgrade overwrites ([`GT-673`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-673)). Registered 2026-08-14 from the Facility benchmark, which ships this loop as a headline capability — repeated review feedback graduated into a deterministic check. **Their guards are zero-dependency scripts; ours is compiled policy with a wasm builtin oracle ([`GT-644`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-644)) — the asset is on our side and the path to the tenant is not.** This is the productised form of "the model proposes, the verifier decides", and without it "the client selects its level" holds only for packs we authored. | `Governance` | Cross | P1 | L | `PENDING` |
| [`GT-674`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-674) | **The AI-execution evidence port and its Langfuse adapter ship with no consumer: no rule reads cost or tokens, and no surface can supply a trace.** `ObservabilityEvidence` models trace id, model, prompt name and version, `costUsd`, `latencyMs`, `totalTokens`, tool calls and evaluation scores (`domain/observability-evidence.ts:20-35`), and `LangfuseEvidenceAdapter` implements `IObservabilityEvidenceSource` (`langfuse-evidence.adapter.ts:26`) and is exported from the package barrel. **Nothing consumes either symbol** — outside those two files, their specs and `index.ts`, a grep across `src` matches nothing: no rule handler, no DI registration in `core-api` or `mcp-server`, no CLI or MCP input that accepts a trace. So no governance rule can decide anything about an AI execution's cost, model or prompt version, which is the entire evidence class the port exists to carry. Registered 2026-08-14 from the Facility benchmark, which gates on exactly this data (per-project budgets at a model gateway, cost attribution per agent and task) — which is what makes wiring the port worth arguing about rather than deleting by default. **The broader candidate row was REFUTED before registration:** the connector is not missing, the consumer is. A port with no consumer reads as shipped capability in every inventory that counts symbols, so the honest alternative — delete it — is a criterion here. | `Core Domain` | Cross | P2 | S | `PENDING` |
| [`GT-672`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-672) | **Every figure that would make the governance claim checkable is derived and committed, and none of it is published where a reader can see it.** The measurements exist and are already kept honest: 666 board rows with statuses and debt economics, a generated maturity reconciliation and executive summary, `iso-5055-mapping.json` with denominators, a classified guard corpus (78 classified, 54 observed failing), all held at a fixed point by `46-validate-derived-artifact-order`. What ships publicly is documentation — `docs.yml` and `docs-release.yml` to `gh-pages` — and none of these figures with it. Registered 2026-08-14 from the Facility benchmark, whose landing page IS its dashboard and is its most transferable asset. **The benchmark also supplied the anti-pattern, measured the same day:** that page renders `0 deterministic guards recorded` and `0h median` — placeholders where the derivation is not wired — so the lesson is the opposite of imitation: publish only figures derived from a committed artifact, each with its denominator, and FAIL the generator when a source is missing rather than rendering a zero. Cheapest positioning move available, because the material is already produced and already guarded. | `Governance` | Cross | P2 | M | `PENDING` |
| [`GT-673`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-673) | **`evolith upgrade` cannot tell a tenant's edit from an upstream change, and overwrites it.** `diffSatelliteVsCore` compares core content against satellite content and emits a `modify` change on ANY difference (`satellite-upgrade-diff.ts:70-81`); `applyChange` then writes the core content over the satellite file unconditionally (`satellite-upgrade-apply.ts:5-15`). **There is no record of what was originally scaffolded** — a grep for `hash`, `checksum`, `fingerprint`, `modified` and `overwrite` across `application/upgrade/*.ts` returns nothing — so "the tenant customised this rule" and "the core changed this rule" are indistinguishable, and the plan the operator approves describes both identically as `Update ruleset: <path>`. `createBackup` bounds the loss to a recoverable copy; it does not prevent the overwrite, and nothing warns that an edit is about to be discarded. Registered 2026-08-14 from the Facility benchmark, which fingerprints its installed template, keeps user-editable files in delimited managed blocks and delivers upgrades as reviewable pull requests. **NOT claimed:** that any tenant has actually lost an edit — nothing runs in production yet ([`GT-435`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-435)/[`GT-448`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-448)), so this is a live defect with no recorded victim. | `CLI` | Cross | P2 | M | `PENDING` |
| [`GT-649`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-649) | **The evaluation corpus carries three documents the loader reports as broken on every load, a phase-gate stage that has evaluated nothing since 2026-07-04, and a logger that doubles every line it writes.** Three faults surfaced together in the same `core-api.log` of CI run `30631939687`, and each is independently real. **(1) Three `*.rules.json` files fail the standard ruleset schema with `must have required property 'rules' / 'principles'` and are skipped every time** — `architecture/topology-recommendation.rules.json` (the ADR-0104 recommendation catalogue, which has its own schema and its own consumer in `TopologyRecommendationService`), and `infrastructure/helm-enforcement.rules.json` + `infrastructure/opa-sidecar-bundle.rules.json` (single-rule declarations enforced by guard `29-validate-opa-sidecar-bundles.mjs` and their paired Rego, not by the rule engine). Nothing was wrong with them: they are different document kinds sharing a suffix, and the loader had no vocabulary to say so. All three additionally failed the schema they *declare*, unnoticed, because nothing ever checked them against it — `severity: "error"` is not in that schema's enum, `name` is not its field (`title` is), the `validation` block it never modelled, and a `$schema` path left stale by the `reference/` reorganisation. **(2) `reference/governance/sdlc/phases/` does not exist**, deleted by `f0d01911` (2026-07-04, *"remove leftover old directories under reference/"*) which removed the whole `reference/governance/` tree; `GT-461` later re-created `gates/` at that same path and not `phases/`. Because `loadGatesForPhase` resolves gates *through* the phase index, `loadAllPhases()` returning `[]` means **every one of the five authored gate files has been unreachable for ~4 weeks** — the phase-gate stage of `SatelliteEvaluationPipeline` produced zero gate results while reporting no error, so a governed evaluation silently skipped the half of the pipeline the SDLC gates live in. **(3) `NestLoggerProvider` forwards its optional `context` unconditionally**, and Nest's `Logger` reads `...optionalParams` positionally, so `warn(msg, undefined)` is not `warn(msg)`: every single-argument log from the domain emitted a paired `WARN undefined` line. Measured on the baseline reproduction: **44 such lines across 11 requests**, doubling the volume of everything the Core writes. | `Core Domain` | Cross | P1 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-648`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-648) | **The deployed Core re-reads and re-validates its entire ruleset corpus on every `POST /api/v1/evaluate`, blocking the Node event loop for the duration.** `RuleEvaluationEngine.discoverAndEvaluate` calls `rulesetRepo.loadAllRulesets(corePath)` on each evaluation, and core-api's `IRulesetRepository` is a bare `DiskRulesetRepository` with no cache — so a directory walk over 176 `*.rules.json` files plus an Ajv pass producing 393 normalized rules runs per request. Both halves are synchronous CPU work inside `await`ed calls, so nothing else on the loop advances while they run. **Observed in CI run `30631939687` (Reliability workflow, artifacts `k6-smoke.json` + `core-api.log`):** the corpus's load-time WARN block repeats once per k6 iteration (~1/s) — a boot-time load would print it once — and **a `GET /health` that landed during that work took 498.5 ms end-to-end while core-api logged its own handler at `durationMs=0`**, with `http_req_waiting` 498.4 ms against `http_req_connecting` 0.17 ms. The request was never slow; it was queued behind a blocked loop. **Reproduced locally 2026-07-31 against `origin/develop` at `3051e499`:** eleven evaluations produced exactly eleven corpus loads (33 ruleset WARNs at 3/load, 11 phase WARNs at 1/load), and one `loadAllRulesets` call was measured at 30.4–54.2 ms with a queued 10 ms timer waiting up to 14 ms. That laptop figure is roughly a tenth of the runner's, and the gap between the two is the point: the same defect on slower, contended I/O is what produced 498 ms. **Also per request, and compounding it:** `SdlcDataLoaderService.loadAllGates` re-reads the phase index once per phase (`loadPhase` → `loadAllPhases`), turning one scan into N+1. **Not addressed by the k6 threshold fix** that asserts the median instead of a 10-sample p95 — that stops the flake gating CI and deliberately leaves this defect in place; it is why `/health` has a multi-hundred-ms tail at 1 VU. | `Core API` | Cross | P1 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-647`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-647) | **Every deployable image lists the workspace packages its runner stage copies BY HAND, so a TRANSITIVE dependency added anywhere in the graph silently produces an image that dies at startup.** `node_modules/@beyondnet/evolith-*` are workspace SYMLINKS into `src/packages/*`, so copying `node_modules` copies the links and not the packages; a link whose target is absent from the hand-written list dangles, and Node fails at `require` time inside the container. **Observed 2026-07-31, reproduced locally on Docker 29.4.3 at the first attempt:** `evolith-core-api:local` crash-loops with `Cannot find module '@beyondnet/evolith-contracts/ingest'`, require stack `infra-providers/dist/tracker/evaluation-ingest.client.js` → `infra-providers/dist/index.js` → `core-api/dist/core-domain.module.js` → `main.js`; `docker run --entrypoint sh evolith-core-api:local -c "ls /repo/src/packages/contracts/dist/"` returns `No such file or directory`. The `exports` map is CORRECT — `"./ingest"` is declared — the files were simply never in the image. **Cause:** GT-604 (#314) added `evaluation-ingest.client.ts`, which imports `@beyondnet/evolith-contracts/ingest` and is re-exported from `infra-providers/dist/index.js`, putting `contracts` on the runtime require graph for the first time. Nobody forgot a DIRECT dependency: at that depth all three Dockerfiles were complete. **Three images were wrong, not two** — `core-api`, `mcp-server` AND `src/sdk/cli`, the third found by the guard rather than by reading. **Blast radius:** the `Chaos drill` job of `reliability.yml` failed on `develop` three consecutive times (runs `30628493705`, `30630994704`, `30632498438`), each time as `[wait-for-target] TIMEOUT after 180s` — and the job captured NO container logs, so the require stack the container printed every time was discarded. **The workflow was also blind to its own cause:** its `paths` filter listed `core-api` and `core-domain` but not `infra-providers` or `contracts`, so it never ran on the commit that broke it and went red later on an unrelated edit. Same failure class as [`GT-625`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-625): a subpath import that resolves through the npm-workspace symlink and breaks in a real install. **Fix:** the closure is now COMPUTED, not read — `56-validate-docker-workspace-closure` walks the transitive workspace graph from each Dockerfile's owning package and fails when the FINAL stage (found by position, not by the name `runner`) omits any member's `dist` or `package.json`; a second check fails a VALUE import of an undeclared workspace package, which is the hoisting trap that would make the first check confidently wrong. **Verified by BOOTING, not by inspecting the diff:** rebuilt, `docker compose -f product/infra/docker-compose.evolith.yml up -d --build redis core-api` reports the container `healthy`, `wait-for-target.sh` exits 0 on a real governed verdict, and the logs contain no `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. The guard was run against the UNFIXED tree and named all three Dockerfiles. Runner evidence for the drill itself is the remaining criterion. **CLOSED on runner evidence, not on a local green.** Run `30638750508` on this branch: `Chaos drill` and `k6 load profile` both green; the step that timed out three times reports `target is serving governed verdicts` 6 seconds after bring-up, and the artifact now carries 103 KB of container logs where it carried none. The mcp-server image was also built and booted (`/health` 200, zero module errors), and `require.resolve` of the failing subpath resolves inside it. The CLI image — the third victim, found by the guard rather than by the briefing — was built and exercised end to end against a mounted satellite: `init --yes` completes and `validate --format json` returns a real envelope, so the graph that reaches `contracts` is genuinely loaded and not merely present. | `Infra` | Cross | P0 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-645`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-645) | **A gap claim is derived from a pull request's PROSE instead of its DIFF, so the guard contests what nobody claimed and misses what the diff actually did.** `50-validate-gap-claim` reads title, body and branch name, and never reads the diff. **False positives, observed first-hand in run `30631939629` on PR #320:** `ids claimed .. 10, contested .. 3` — `GT-583`, `GT-602` and `GT-643` reported as contested between #320 and #324, when #324 is the pull request that *documents* this defect and claims none of them; six more (`GT-578`, `GT-582`, `GT-588`, `GT-591`, `GT-642`, `GT-644`) attributed to #324 because its evidence table names them, and `GT-609` to #320 from a generator docstring. Nine attributions, honest count zero. Fourth and fifth occurrence in one day, after #316 (flagged for GT-583 over a sentence saying it belongs to another PR) and #317 (flagged for GT-602 over a paragraph saying this change did not break it). The printed remedy — "decide which pull request owns the id" — has no work to do; what authors do instead is delete the sentence, and #316's body was edited to remove a true cross-reference. **False negative, the half that survives #324 and the reason this row is separate from it:** #324 merged into `develop` on 2026-07-31 (`4c785c5c`) and narrows the prose rule — ids now count from the title, the branch, or after an explicit `Closes`/`Fixes`/`Advances` marker — which fixes the noise. It does not make the diff an input, so this row is what remains, not a duplicate of it. **PR #315 — "Board reconciliation: credit the eleven gaps this wave merged", branch `chore/reconcile-board-wave` — flipped ELEVEN rows to `DONE` and wrote eleven closure-evidence records with no `GT-*` in title or branch.** Under the narrowed rule it claims nothing, and it is the pull request that moved the board most in this wave. **This row's own registering pull request is the second instance, and it is self-demonstrating:** it flips eight rows and adds six closure records, and the guard reads from it exactly one claim — `GT-645`, the row it merely REGISTERS — and none of the six it actually closes. **Measured on a runner, not predicted:** run `30633517559` reports `ids claimed .. 1`, `mentioned, not claimed 10`, `contested .. 0`, and exits ✓. Both halves are visible in that one line — #324 took the contested count from 3 to 0, and the pull request that closes six gaps claims none of them. Confirmed against the merged implementation: `claimedIds()` builds its set from `pr.title`, `pr.headRefName` and marker-prefixed body clauses, and the file contains no reference to a diff, a file list or a patch at all. **One briefed instance was checked and REFUTED rather than registered:** #314 was said to have flipped GT-602 and written its closure record; #314 touches twelve files, none under `reference/core/control-center/`, and GT-602 was closed by #306 on branch `feat/gt-602-close-abac-wasm-parity` — a branch name the guard reads correctly. The class is real; that instance was not. | `Governance` | Cross | P2 | M | `DONE` |
| [`GT-644`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-644) | **A policy can call an OPA builtin the shipped wasm runtime cannot execute, and every check in the repository stays green.** `compile-opa-wasm.mjs` builds `src/rulesets/opa/` into `policy.wasm`; `OpaEvaluator` loads it through `@open-policy-agent/opa-wasm`. Some builtins are compiled natively into the module, the rest are HOST-DISPATCHED into JavaScript, and the SDK implements exactly six of those. **A policy calling any other one passes `opa test`, passes `opa build`, then throws** `not implemented: built-in function 24: time.parse_rfc3339_ns` at evaluation time — which `evaluateAll`'s catch turns into `OPA engine error — enforcement blocked` for **EVERY rule in the run** (`opa-evaluator.ts:190-198`). One builtin in one policy fails the whole OPA half of the gate, reported as an infrastructure hiccup. **Coverage before this row was exactly one policy** — `probabilistic-evidence-rule.handler.spec.ts` asserts one `.rego` matches no `time.*`; the other 33 the bundle links were unguarded. **Prevention, not a live defect:** a scan on 2026-07-31 found no other offender and the current bundle dispatches only `sprintf`. **Two measurements refuted the obvious design.** The SDK's `capabilities.json` is not an oracle — it lists `time.diff` as supported (it is host-dispatched and unimplemented) and omits `object.keys` (it is native and works), wrong in both directions. And reachability is OPA's to decide: `json.patch` is host-dispatched and unimplemented at **83 sites**, all in `*.test.rego` that OPA prunes, so a scan blind to pruning opens red with 83 false positives. **BUILT:** `55-validate-wasm-builtin-support.mjs` reads the compiled bundle's own `builtins()` table as the authority — no list to maintain, no false positives — and adds a curated, explicitly PARTIAL set that turns the common cases red from the `.rego` with `file:line`, no compiler and no network, over the import closure of the entrypoints only. Entrypoints and bundle directory are parsed out of the build script rather than restated. Observed red by guard 43 in 29ms; both checks disarmed one at a time and exactly the test pinning each turned red. **Observed green on a runner** (run `30630122256`) — but only after the first attempt SKIPPED both steps without running them: `bash -e` stops the job at the first failure and `50-validate-gap-claim` failed ahead of them, partly on this PR's own body "claiming" GT-578 by naming the job it wires into. The steps now run before the three that ask the network. **CLOSED 2026-07-31:** merged into `develop` as #323 (`a4914d92`), a rebase of the superseded #321, with all seven required contexts green. The pull request #321 was closed rather than merged, pointing at #323. | `Governance` | Cross | P1 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-643`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-643) | **`evolith agents install --dry-run` writes to disk — the flag is declared, documented and never read.** `agents.command.ts:647` declares `-d, --dry-run`, `AgentsCommandOptions.dryRun` types it, and `installAgent()` never consults it: it calls `this.registry.installAgent(process.cwd(), config, rulesetContent)` unconditionally (`agents.command.ts:218`). **`init`, `upgrade` and `adr` all read theirs** — `init` swaps in a `DryRunFileSystem`, `upgrade` returns before applying — so this is one command out of step with the convention, not a missing convention. **PROVEN, not inferred:** running `test/agents.e2e-spec.ts -t "dry-run"` alone against a clean tree leaves three TRACKED files modified — `src/sdk/cli/rulesets/agents/agents-registry.json` and both files under `rulesets/agents/test-value/`. **The test output is committed as if it were product data:** `test-value` is the string the prompt mock returns for every question (`test/mock-prompt.service.ts:11-12`), and `6bb43cfa` versioned that agent into the repository's own rulesets. **A dry run that writes is worse than no dry run**, because it is the flag a user reaches for precisely when they do not want to be trusted with the real one — and the surface conformance tester never caught it, because no oracle asks whether `--dry-run` left the disk unchanged. **FIXED 2026-07-30:** the dry branch never reaches the writer at all — rather than writing through a no-op filesystem, which regresses the moment the writer grows a new call — reports the paths it would have created via a new `planInstall`, and the menu path now forwards the caller's options, since `evolith agents --dry-run` with no subcommand used to drop them and write. The three committed artifacts are deleted, the e2e suite runs in a temp directory, and its oracle now ASSERTS an unchanged disk plus a contrast case proving a real install still writes: `did not crash` passed for as long as the defect existed. **Survey recorded:** `init`, `upgrade`, `adr`, `scaffold`, `docs`, `fixtures` and `generate-domain` all gate their writes on the flag — `agents` was the only declaration in the CLI with no reader. **LAST CRITERION CLOSED 2026-07-31 by #320 (`b5545f31`):** the cross-surface tester now covers the CLASS, not just this instance — `src/tests/exploration/no-effect-contracts.ts` declares each contract as an invocation, the state that must not move, and a CONTRAST invocation that must move it, and `runner.ts` runs the no-effect phase in a fresh module per invocation. The contrast is what stops the oracle going green precisely when a command has stopped working altogether. **Deleting the residue exposed two hidden dependents on it**, both repaired here: `47-validate-joined-paths` anchored a generated-subtree claim on `src/sdk/cli/rulesets` existing — true in a clean checkout only because the residue was inside it — and went red naming `policy.wasm`; and one resolver spec still read the real tree, so its EACCES simulation stopped firing. Verified by parking the build artifact: 101 suites / 1447 tests green with the directory absent. | `CLI` | Cross | P1 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-642`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-642) | **No check anywhere fails on a runtime import cycle, and the two that existed were found by hand.** [`GT-641`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-641) removed two real `require` cycles from `core-domain` — they had reached a 346-module package unobserved, because the measurement that finds them runs nowhere. `lint:boundaries` polices layer direction, not cyclicity; `tsc` compiles a cycle without complaint; no governance guard reads the module graph at all. **The measurement already exists and is not reachable:** [`GT-589`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-589) ships `@beyondnet/evolith-repo-facts` plus `findImportCycles`, and both live only on `feat/gt-589-repo-facts` (PR #309). **The block is lifted and the guard is built. CLOSED 2026-07-31.** #309 landed, so `.harness/scripts/ci/54-validate-import-cycles.mjs` loads `extractTypeScriptFacts` from `@beyondnet/evolith-repo-facts` and `findImportCycles` from core-domain — GT-589's extractor, not a second detector beside it. The package list is DERIVED from the root `package.json` workspace globs rather than hand-written, so a new package is swept the day it is added. Type-only cycles are reported separately and do not fail the build, because conflating them would push people toward `import type` as an evasion. Wired as "No runtime import cycle in any workspace package" in `Governance guards (GT-578)`, with the extractor built in an earlier step, and exercised by `43-validate-guard-negative-fixtures`. **It should sweep every package, not just the one already measured** — `cli`, `mcp-server`, `core`, `agent-runtime` and `sdk-client` have never been through the extractor, and the barrel-owns-the-type shape that produced both GT-641 cycles is not specific to `core-domain`. | `Governance` | Cross | P2 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-641`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-641) | **The Core's own sources carry two runtime import cycles, and nothing that runs in CI today can see them.** [`GT-589`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-589)'s structural fact base, pointed at `src/packages/core-domain`, reports `application/services/index.ts` ⇄ `application/use-cases/initialize-project.use-case.ts` and `application/validators/blocking-criteria-validator.ts` ⇄ `application/validators/phase-gate-validator.service.ts`. **Neither is type-only**, so neither is erased at compile time: both are real `require` cycles, and the fact base classifies them as such because it distinguishes type-only edges from value edges. **One shape, twice:** a module publishes a type AND constructs or re-exports the collaborator that consumes it, so the collaborator has to import back through it to name its own parameters. **The two-node chains the report names are the smaller half of the finding** — the strongly connected components are wider: `project-scaffolder.service` and `phase-transition.use-case` sit in the first, `evidence-validator` and `ruleset-loader` in the second, so a fix confined to the two files named in the chain would have moved the cycle rather than removed it. **FIXED 2026-07-30:** the shared shapes move to `application/services/use-case.types.ts` and `application/validators/phase-gate-validator.types.ts`; both former owners re-export them, so every existing import path is untouched — including `@beyondnet/evolith-core-domain/application/services`, which the CLI and the MCP server import `InitProjectInput` from. **THE GUARD IS NOT BUILT, and stating why matters more than the fix:** `@beyondnet/evolith-repo-facts` exists only on `feat/gt-589-repo-facts` (PR #309) and is absent from `main`, so no check on the default branch can run this measurement. Until #309 lands, a new cycle regresses silently exactly as these two did. | `Core` | Cross | P2 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-640`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-640) | **The guard compared the snapshot against six numbers copied out of the snapshot, so it could only ever pass — and the file it was failing to guard is stamped onto 388 rows of a larger artifact.** `native-evaluability-snapshot.json` declared in its own header that it was "a CAPTURED SNAPSHOT, not the source of truth". **No capture script existed.** It was hand-maintained and it drifted: it pinned `documentation-only: 129` while Core pinned 136, and went on calling rules `unimplemented-native` after they had handlers. Its guard in `iso-5055-mapping.test.mjs` asserted the snapshot's six class counts against six numbers typed into the test — the same six literals the snapshot already contained. **Expected value and actual value were copies of each other**, so the assertion held for as long as the file was unchanged, whatever Core actually pinned; it reported an agreement it never checked, which is worse than no guard, because the row it protects reads as verified. **The drift does not stay where it starts.** `build-iso-5055-mapping.mjs` stamps `nativeEvaluability` onto EVERY row of the ISO/IEC 5055 mapping from that file — 388 rows after the recapture, 381 before — so one stale class is laundered into a derived artifact five times the size of its input, and overstates the handler backlog that is [`GT-598`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-598)'s entire deliverable. The recapture→rebuild order was written down nowhere, and the mapping's own guard ran in **no workflow at all**. **FIXED 2026-07-29:** a capture script that runs Core's REAL triage through `ts-node` instead of reimplementing the classification, the measurement extracted out of the jest spec into `test/rule-corpus-triage.ts` so a script can reach it — that unreachability is why the file was maintained by hand — snapshot-vs-fresh-triage assertions in both directions, a documentation-job guard that reads Core's pinned counts OUT of the spec and **throws** when the literal is missing or reshaped, and both steps declared as links in the [`GT-630`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-630) derived-artifact chain. **Recaptured on 2026-07-29 after merging the fix into the current line, and the drift had grown while it was in flight:** `native-handler` 139 → 151, `documentation-only` 129 → 136, the handler backlog 60 → **48**, corpus 379 → 386, mapping 381 → 388 rows. Twelve of those handlers were closed by [`GT-595`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-595) and [`GT-632`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-632) while the hand-maintained snapshot went on reporting them as backlog — the defect demonstrating itself one more time, on numbers nobody typed twice. **RENUMBERED GT-633 -> GT-640 on 2026-07-30**, after `49-validate-gap-id-allocation` found that `GT-633` already named a different gap on `develop` (`evolith init` scaffolding, `c8270e48`, registered a day earlier). The newer row moves, per the guard's own advice. | `Governance` | Cross | P1 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-639`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-639) | **Nothing makes in-flight work visible across branches, so the same gap gets fixed twice.** On 2026-07-30 the same work was done twice, three separate times, by sessions that could not see each other: [`GT-640`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-640) (registered as GT-633 at the time) fixed by a standalone capture script on `main` (#276) AND by an independent in-spec renderer on `develop` (#279) — both correct, both recapturing the same numbers, and two generators for one artifact is GT-640's own defect one level up, so a third session spent a full reconciliation collapsing them; the evidence guard's parser fixed twice (`d1ea72a3` on main, `5acb29ed` on develop); and a GT id allocated twice, forcing the renumber behind [`GT-638`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-638). **The cost is not the merge conflict** — it is the duplicated work before anyone reached it, plus the reconciliation, which itself introduced a defect (an order-sensitive comparison that rewrote the capture date every run, invisible for a day because both runs stamped the same date). A row carries a status but not WHO is working it or WHERE, so two sessions can both read `PENDING` and both start, correctly. **The single-driver convention already exists; what is missing is any mechanism that makes it observable**, so on a busy day it degrades silently. **GUARD BUILT 2026-07-30 — three criteria of four.** `50-validate-gap-claim` derives the claim set from OPEN PULL REQUESTS (every `GT-*` in a PR's title, body or branch name) and fails when one id is claimed by two, naming both with their branches. Derived rather than hand-written on purpose: a hand-written claim goes stale in the direction that matters. Wired into `Governance guards (GT-578)` with `GH_TOKEN`; observed red by `43-validate-guard-negative-fixtures` (39/39). **Criterion 1 is deliberately still open:** a claim list committed to the board would derive from live GitHub state and be stale the moment anyone opened a PR — and [`GT-630`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-630)'s chain exists to insist derived artifacts reach a fixed point, so a perpetually-stale one inside it would be worse than none. It also cannot see a branch with no open PR, and says so in its own failure text. | `Governance` | Cross | P2 | M | `DONE` |
| [`GT-646`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-646) | **A CI gate asserted `p(95)` over TEN samples, so one request decided it — and when it fired it took the profile that follows it down too.** `smoke.js` runs 1 VU x 10 iterations and asserted `health_latency p(95) < 150`. k6 computes p(95) at index `0.95*(n-1) = 8.55`, interpolating 55 % of the way from the 9th sorted sample to the slowest, so over 10 samples "p95" is the max wearing a percentile's name. **Observed in run `30631939687`, on a tree that was green 20 minutes earlier** (run `30631081684`, same commit `d97db1d4`): `x 'p(95)<150' p(95)=276.1ms` with every other threshold passing — checks 50/50, `evaluate_latency` p95 122 ms, 0 % errors, 0 % 429s. Reconstructed from the run's own artifact: **9 health samples between 2.72 ms and 4.3 ms, and one at 498.5 ms**, where 276.1 ms is exactly `4.3 + 0.55*(498.5 - 4.3)` — **a latency no request in that run ever had**. Reproduced against k6 v2.0.0 by feeding it those ten values, which returns `p(95)=276.1ms` to the decimal. **The obvious cause was checked and REFUTED: this is not cold start**, so neither a warm-up iteration nor a raised ceiling would have fixed it. `wait-for-target.sh` drives a full governed evaluation before k6 starts; `evaluate_latency` was flat across all ten iterations (min 107.3 ms, max 122.4 ms) when the ~107-rule OPA-wasm path is where coldness would show first; and core-api logged the health handler at `durationMs=0` while k6 measured **498.4 ms of `http_req_waiting`** against 0.17 ms of connect. The request queued behind a blocked event loop: core-api re-scans and re-validates its whole ruleset corpus on EVERY evaluation, visible as the same WARN block repeating once per iteration in `core-api.log`. **That re-scan is a real performance defect, is deliberately NOT fixed here, and is registered separately.** **A second red step followed from the first:** `report-summary.mjs` crashed with `ENOENT: k6-average.json` — the average-load profile is gated behind smoke and legitimately never ran — turning one threshold miss into two failures and replacing the smoke result that explained it with a stack trace. **CLOSED 2026-07-31.** Smoke now asserts `med` against the SAME `SLO.*` numbers: no ceiling was raised, and a median under the p95 ceiling is implied by the SLO — a strictly weaker claim than the SLO, and the strongest one n=10 can support. `p(95)`/`p(99)` stay ENFORCED in `average-load.js`, which collects thousands of samples; the tail is still measured and published on smoke, it just no longer gates. `report-summary.mjs` now reads each threshold back out of the run instead of re-declaring it — otherwise it would print `health p95 < 150 -> FAIL` on a smoke run that passed — and reports "did not run" and exits 0 when a summary is absent. **Verified by A/B against a stub reproducing the distribution** (p95 273.7 ms, max 495 ms): the pre-fix script exits 99 and the post-fix one exits 0 with the tail unchanged, and a genuinely slow endpoint (200 ms on every request) still fails `x 'med<150' med=199.83ms` — the gate kept its teeth. | `Reliability` | Cross | P2 | XS | `DONE` |
| [`GT-638`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-638) | **The board has no id allocator, so two parallel branches hand out the same GT number.** An id is chosen by reading the highest `GT-*` on the branch you happen to be on, and nothing contrasts that with any other branch — so two sessions in parallel allocate the same number and one finds out at merge time. It already happened: `8449af3d` on `develop` reads *"renumber the ratchet fix GT-634 -> GT-637, ID collision with develop"*; that row and [`GT-634`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-634) on `main` took the same id within hours, from sessions that could not see each other. **The renumber is manual and lossy**, which costs more than the clash: an id lives in the board row, the catalog anchor, the closure-evidence record, cross-references in both languages, commit messages and PR bodies — and only the first three are mechanically checkable. `08-validate-tracking` cannot help by construction: it validates within one working tree, and branches are outside its world. Not an isolated slip either — in the 2026-07-30 `develop` → `main` merge the same convergent-duplication pattern appears three times: GT-640 (then GT-633) fixed twice, the evidence guard's parser fixed twice, this id allocated twice. **CLOSED 2026-07-30 — and on its first real run it found a SECOND collision nobody knew about.** `49-validate-gap-id-allocation` compares every id's **Title:** on HEAD against the title the same id carries on the base branch; a different title on the same id is one number naming two gaps. Against `origin/develop` it reported **`GT-633`**: there it is "`evolith init` scaffolds a repository that cannot pass its own governance on the first run" (`c8270e48`, 2026-07-29), on main it was the tautological-guard row (`c3221276`, 2026-07-30), since renumbered to `GT-640`. The merge had not surfaced it. It refuses to guess which side is wrong — a retitle and a collision look identical from outside — so it names both. Wired into `Governance guards (GT-578)` with an explicit `git fetch` of the base, since a shallow CI checkout lacks it. Observed red by `43-validate-guard-negative-fixtures` (38/38). Still cannot see an id allocated on a branch with no open PR, and its `--verbose` output says so on every newly allocated id. | `Governance` | Cross | P2 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-637`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-637) | **The GT-578 dead-reference ratchet was stuck at 305 by two real bugs in the GUARD, not by the size of the evidence-record backlog it claimed to measure.** Fixing the PR that closed `GT-633` surfaced a `Governance guards (GT-578)` failure and, investigating it, an uncommitted, unfinished refactor already sitting in the working tree: `resolveCommand` called `npmScriptOperand(...)`, a function that was never defined, so the guard crashed with a `ReferenceError` on every invocation — even in plain report mode. Repairing that function (restoring, then correcting its behaviour) dropped the count from 309 to 78, which exposed the second bug: the corpus uses `npm run --workspace <path> <script>` throughout — the `--workspace` flag BEFORE the script name, a valid form `npm` accepts — and the resolver's script-name search had no notion that `--workspace` consumes the next token, so it mistook the workspace path itself for the script name and reported it undeclared. Both bugs were pre-existing; they were only VISIBLE together, because before the first fix the guard never got far enough to reach the second. **CLOSED 2026-07-29.** Both bugs fixed, dropping 309 → 52. The remaining ~290 pre-`src/`-refactor and pre-`product/`-move paths this job's own docstring names (`sdk/cli`, `apps/core-api`, `packages/*`, `reference/infrastructure/`, `reference/operations/`, the retired `@evolith/*` scope) were a DATA migration, not a code fix, and were already partially done in the uncommitted state found in the tree; the rest were completed the same way — 18 further substitutions, each verified against a real successor file on disk before being written, never guessed. That took 52 → 22. One more, `chat.command.ts`, was a real `wc -l` missing only its directory prefix (`src/sdk/cli/src/commands/chat/chat.command.ts`), confirmed to exist and closing 22 → 21. A third, unrelated defect was found and fixed on the way: `.harness/bin/opa` (15 of the original 309) is gitignored and downloaded by a separate CI step — same finding as `GT-632` on guard 47, a gitignored artifact absent on a clean checkout being mistaken for a wrong path — so `resolveCommand` now reports it `unchecked`, not `dead`, for any path under `.harness/bin/` or `dist/`. `--max-dead` moved from 305 to **21**, proven exact by observing the guard fail at 20. The residual 21 is genuinely retired or narrative, not a rename: a consolidated `.bmad-core/AGENTS.md` replacing per-agent files (2 records), the retired `evolith-bff` gateway (3), the retired `packages/mcp-tools` (2), two retired k8s manifests whose successors have different content entirely rather than a moved path (3), a retired sandbox app (1), three root-level `npm test`/`npm run info`/`npm run test:cov` invocations from before workspaces existed with no root-level successor (7), and three records where `vision/` or `tools/list` — an MCP JSON-RPC method name, not a filesystem path — are prose describing history, misparsed as path-shaped tokens (3). None of these were force-mapped to a plausible-looking target; a wrong rename would have been worse than an honest residual. 39/39 guard self-tests pass, including new coverage for both bugs and both negative twins (`--workspace` script-check still catches a truly undeclared script; a non-gitignored missing binary is still `dead`) so the fix cannot blind the guard the way it was meant not to. `08-validate-tracking`, `42`–`45` in the same job, and a full `--execute --verbose` run (report mode, pre-existing failures on 6 unrelated records, none among the paths touched here) all confirmed clean. | `.harness` | Cross | P1 | S  | `DONE` |
| [`GT-636`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-636) | **Three CI checks were red on PR #275 — `Security Audit`, `E2E Tests`, `Evolith Core Validation` — and all three were real, fixable defects, not the accepted debt any of them first looked like.** `Evolith Core Validation` failed at `Bilingual Terminology Lint` (2 of 5 flagged terms were genuine untranslated prose; the other 3 were table/citation cells where translating only the flagged term would have made the document less consistent). `E2E Tests` failed on 1 of 132 tests calling two CLI flags (`--architecture --arch-level F1`) that never existed — the real flag is `-a, --arch`. `Security Audit` reported 31 `npm audit` findings (29 high, 2 moderate); investigation traced them to a SINGLE root cause — a `brace-expansion` DoS advisory (`GHSA-mh99-v99m-4gvg`, affecting every version `<=5.0.7`, patched only in `5.0.8`, published after this repo's existing `overrides.brace-expansion: "2.1.2"` was set) propagating severity up through the entire jest/eslint/`@nestjs/cli` devDependency toolchain, plus one independent `fast-uri` finding. **CLOSED 2026-07-29, all three.** Terminology and E2E fixed as described in their own prior closure note. `Security Audit`: root `overrides.brace-expansion` bumped `2.1.2` → `5.0.8`, plus a new `overrides.fast-uri: "3.1.4"` (in-range of `ajv`'s own `^3.0.1`) — a 2-line `package.json` diff. Getting there required real forensics: a plain `npm install` left 4 of the 10 tree positions pinned to a STALE lockfile-recorded version regardless of the new override, requiring the specific `packages` entries to be deleted from `package-lock.json` before a fresh `npm install` would honor the override at all; a SCOPED nested override (`"eslint": {"brace-expansion": ...}`) made the fix WORSE, not better — once a package has a nested override object for even one unrelated dependency (`ajv`), npm stops the ROOT-level unscoped rule from cascading into that subtree at all, so the working fix is the plain top-level override with NO nested entry competing for the same package. 0 high / 0 critical confirmed with the exact CI command (`npm audit --audit-level=high`, exit 0) in an isolated clean environment (a scratch worktree with a real, non-symlinked `node_modules`, since this session's own earlier per-package symlink farm was independently confirmed to corrupt dependency-graph tooling). 2 moderate remain, deliberately not chased: a Windows-only path-traversal in `@hono/node-server`'s `serve-static`, irrelevant to this repo's Linux CI/deployment, whose fix requires bumping an EXACT-PINNED production dependency (`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`) rather than a devDependency override. Verified beyond the audit: full `core-domain` (1442/1442), CLI unit (1436/1436) and E2E (132/132, rebuilding every workspace dependency first, exactly as CI's own job does) and `mcp-server` (433/437, 4 pre-existing skips) suites all green against the fixed tree. | `Cross` | Cross | P2 | S  | `DONE` |
| [`GT-635`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-635) | **The dead-reference ratchet has been over budget on main since 2026-07-28, so the governance job is permanently red.** `41-validate-evidence-commands --strict --max-dead 305` reports **309 dead references on a clean checkout**, so `Governance guards (GT-578)` exits 1 on every run of `ci-cd.yml` — observed on runs `30482497995`, `30470648146` and `30466280865`, all on `main` with nothing unmerged. The budget was set in `4a1b92d6` "where it was measured"; ten commits have edited `gap-closure-evidence.json` since, each adding `validationCommands`, and the count drifted past it. The ratchet's whole purpose is that dead references must not GROW, and it is reporting exactly that while being ignored, because a permanently red job reads as background noise — the same failure mode [`GT-622`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-622) records for the orphaned CodeQL configuration. The next change that genuinely adds a dead reference will be indistinguishable from the four already there. **What not to do:** raise the budget to 309, which converts a breached ratchet into a ratified one. | `Governance` | Cross | P2 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-634`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-634) | **The published CLI and MCP resolve an SDK build that predates the security wave, because a caret range under 1.x pins it.** `@beyondnet/evolith-cli@1.2.1` and `@beyondnet/evolith-mcp@1.2.1` both declare `@beyondnet/evolith-sdk: ^1.1.0`, and that resolves to **exactly `1.1.0`** — the SDK publishes `1.0.0`, `1.1.0` and `2.0.0` and nothing in between, and a caret cannot cross a major. The dates are the finding: `sdk@1.1.0` was published 2026-07-18, `sdk@2.0.0` on 2026-07-27 as part of the release that closed [`GT-570`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-570), and both consumers on 2026-07-28 — **after** 2.0.0 existed, still on the 1.x line. So the exposure GT-570 declared closed for "anyone installing `latest`" is not closed for this dependency. Stated precisely, because the stronger claim is unverified here: what is measured is the dates and the resolution, not the contents — this row does not assert that `sdk@1.1.0` carries a specific vulnerability. Found while executing [`GT-624`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-624)'s deprecation criterion, which is what exposed the range: deprecating `sdk@1.1.0` would warn on every fresh install of the current line with no 1.x successor to offer.  **CLOSED 2026-07-30 — 1.2.2 is published, and the security framing of this row was WRONG.** A clean install of `@beyondnet/evolith-cli@latest` now resolves `@beyondnet/evolith-sdk@2.0.0` with the binary booting. But verifying criterion 2 refuted the premise: **none of the seven security-wave commits touches `src/packages/sdk-client` — zero files** — and the wave's own release commit names the three packages it affected (mcp, cli, agent-runtime). The SDK was republished alongside them, not fixed. What was real is a stale MAJOR pinned by a caret under 1.x plus a lockfile that nested the registry tarball where it shadowed the workspace link — the `GT-625` class, not a security exposure. The hedge written in when the row was opened is what made the correction cheap. | `Infra` | Cross | P1 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-633`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-633) | **`evolith init` produced a repository that fails its own first `evolith validate`, and only the GT-595 handlers made it visible.** With `MTN-05` and `GIT-08` given real handlers by the config-shaped slice, a freshly scaffolded satellite reported two blocking findings that were REAL verdicts rather than the GT-595 blocking-and-skipped invariant: the scaffold declared no `spec.boundedContexts`, and it wrote no commitlint configuration at all. The second is the sharper one — the corpus MANDATES Conventional Commits (`GIT-08`, `MUST`, `blocking`) and the product's own bootstrap command shipped a repository where nothing enforced them. Both were pinned by name in `rule-applicability.integration.spec.ts` precisely so that closing them would be visible. **CLOSED 2026-07-29, and the two halves were closed by OPPOSITE means, because they are not the same kind of defect.** `GIT-08` was a scaffold gap and nothing else: Conventional Commits bind from the first commit and the corpus already states the convention and its seven types verbatim, so there was nothing to decide and nothing to invent. `init` now emits all three legs the handler reads — `commitlint.config.mjs`, the commitlint packages in `devDependencies`, and (with `--features hooks`) a `.husky/commit-msg` running `npx --no-install commitlint`, so a missing tool is an ERROR rather than the silent skip `GT-623` found. Without `hooks` the config is still written and the result carries a warning saying nothing runs it, rather than leaving a file that looks like enforcement and is not. It is runtime-independent on purpose: commitlint is a Node tool, so a .NET or Python satellite gets a tooling-only `package.json` — which is what such repositories carry in practice and what the handler actually reads; emitting the config alone would have left the rule failing for every non-Node runtime. **`MTN-05` was NOT a scaffold gap, and emitting a `spec.boundedContexts` stanza would have been the wrong fix.** The rule's own description says the strategy MUST be defined "before Phase 2 Design", and a phase-0 scaffold has no bounded contexts to declare; satisfying it at `init` would have meant writing an INVENTED persistence decision into every new repository, after which `MTN-05` would pass for a reason nobody chose. Worse, per the handler's own note the two vocabularies are irreconcilable — `evolith-yaml.schema.json` restricts `persistence` to a database-engine enum under `additionalProperties: false` — so any value written either breaks the contract schema or cannot match the rule as worded. It carries `appliesFromSdlcPhase: 2` instead, which is the applicability fact the rule always stated in prose, and follows the `TAX-06` precedent (annotation plus a parallel rego). The rule is DEFERRED, not weakened, and that is asserted rather than assumed: a test walks phases 0→3 and requires it to bind again at Design. **A methodological finding worth more than the fix.** The premise did not reproduce on first contact: the suite resolves `@beyondnet/evolith-core-domain` through the workspace symlink to `dist/`, which was nine days stale, so both rules were reported `skipped` and the pinned assertion ALREADY read `[]`. A green run in that state would have "proved" the gap closed before a line was written — the same class as `GT-625` and `GT-632`, a pass that depends on untracked build state. It was reproduced only after re-anchoring `node_modules` at the worktree and rebuilding, and each rule was then verified individually rather than through the suite: `GIT-08` executes and PASSES (typescript and python), `MTN-05` is reported `notApplicable` rather than silently passing. | `Core Domain` | Cross | P1 | S  | `DONE` |
| [`GT-632`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-632) | **The MCP denied every tool in production from a path typo, and the evidence that it worked was untracked local state.** `abac-evaluator.ts:322` resolved the compiled policy at `<core>/sdk/cli/rulesets/opa/policy.wasm` — the PRE-`src/`-refactor path. The file lives at `src/sdk/cli/…`. When the stat fails the evaluator denies fail-closed in production, so on a clean checkout with `NODE_ENV=production` the compiled policy never loads and EVERY MCP tool is refused. It looked green because `policy.wasm` is gitignored: a stale file left at the old path by an earlier era made the load succeed locally, and the test that covers it passed against an artifact no fresh checkout would have. Same shape as `GT-625` — a green that depended on untracked state — but on the authorization path. Found on 2026-07-29 while investigating why `abac-rego-parity.spec.ts` had gone red; the rego itself was correct (`opa eval` returns `allow: true` for an architect in production). **`40-validate-path-literals` cannot see this class:** it scans string literals, and this is a `path.join(corePath, 'sdk', 'cli', …)` construction. Twelve such joins survive the refactor across `src/**` — the other eleven are in `opa-input-builder.ts` and `cli-release-rule.handler.ts`, where a wrong path produces a false NEGATIVE in an evaluator rather than a denial, which is quieter and no less wrong. **FIXED for the authorization path on 2026-07-29:** the evaluator now tries both locations, newest first — not sloppiness, but what keeps an already-deployed image working while the path is corrected. Reverting the fix turns `abac-rego-parity.spec.ts` red (1 of 11); with it, mcp-server is 434/434. The other eleven joins and the guard blind spot remain. **Criterion 2 CLOSED 2026-07-29 — and two of the eleven were wrong in a way the prefix alone would not have fixed.** Eight were plain survivors of the `src/` move and took the prefix. The other three were worse: two pointed at `sdk/cli/src/core/mcp/server.ts`, a file that left the CLI entirely when mcp-server became its own package (now `src/packages/mcp-server/src/mcp/mcp-server.service.ts`); and one asserted a per-package `package-lock.json`, which npm WORKSPACES forbids — there is exactly one lockfile, at the root, so that check could only ever be false. A rule nobody can satisfy is not a stricter rule. **The specs and parity fixtures encoded the old layout too** (`cli-release-readiness.fixture.json`, `mcp.fixture.json`), which is why they had stayed green over paths that do not exist: the fixture and the code agreed with each other and neither agreed with the repository. Both are corrected. core-domain 1367/1367, `28-native-evaluator-parity` exit 0. **Criterion 3 CLOSED 2026-07-29 — the guard's first run was mostly wrong, which is the finding.** `47-validate-joined-paths` reported 19 broken paths; only 3 were defects. The other 16 would each have been made WORSE by a fix: six had base `root`, a PARAMETER naming the customer workspace under evaluation, so correcting them would break evaluation for every satellite; nine were members of FALLBACK CHAINS that are correct when one of four layouts resolves, and the natural way to silence those is to delete the fallbacks, breaking deployed images; one was a PROHIBITION whose absence IS the passing state, so correcting it would have inverted the rule. Chains are now detected structurally (array siblings, or the arms of a ternary); prohibitions cannot be, so they carry a written reason. The three real defects: `opa-evaluator.ts` resolved `policy.wasm` and the input schemas without the `src/` prefix, same fail-closed class as the P0 above; `satellite-upgrade-diff.ts` probed `rulesets` behind an early return, reporting zero ruleset changes on every satellite upgrade; `artifact-path-resolver.ts` named an `adr-matrix.json` present in no layout. 11 negative fixtures, both anti-vacuous floors, and `43-validate-guard-negative-fixtures` OBSERVED it red across 36 guards. A known leniency (two joins as sibling arguments) is recorded in its header rather than hidden. | `MCP Server` | Cross | P0 | M  | `DONE` |
| [`GT-630`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-630) | **Derived artifacts have a dependency ORDER and nothing enforced it — it cost three red required checks on 2026-07-28 alone.** `generate-executive-summary.mjs` reads `maturity-reconciliation.json`, which is itself derived from the gap board. Generate the summary BEFORE reconciling and it captures a value the reconciler is about to move. Each artifact's own `--check` then passes at that moment — the summary genuinely matches what was just written — so `ci-runner governance` goes green locally and `Validate documentation` goes red on the runner, where the steps run in the declared order. That is not a stale-artifact bug, which the individual `--check` modes already catch; it is an ORDER bug, invisible to any check that looks at one artifact at a time. The correct sequence (reconcile → suite → generate → validate) was written down nowhere in the repository, which is why the same mistake was made three times in one session by someone who had already been bitten by it twice. **CLOSED on registration.** The chain is now DATA in `46-validate-derived-artifact-order.mjs` — producer, what it consumes, what it writes — walked in dependency order, wired into `Validate documentation`. It reports the FIRST stale link with the command that fixes it and the inputs that must be current before it, because an artifact built on stale input is not independently wrong. Writing the tests bounded what the fixed-point pass is worth, and the bound is recorded in the guard: with deterministic generators, currency-in-order already subsumes it; it earns its place on a generator whose output does not depend only on its declared inputs, which is the case the self-test exercises. 8 self-tests, four of them anti-vacuous. Registered in `guard-classification.mjs`; 58 guards classified, 35 observed failing. | `Governance` | Cross | P2 | S | `DONE` |

Found on 2026-07-28 while auditing why 24 rows were in progress. A row with no criteria is worse than an unmet one: it cannot be finished, cannot be measured, and reads as active work. **CLOSED 2026-07-28.** The guard already refused a DONE row with an unticked criterion; it now also refuses a non-DONE row with NO criteria to tick, in either language, naming the row, its status and the language. Scope is every open row including DEFERRED, epics and milestones — deliberately, since an aggregator row is the easiest place for prose to hide. It prints its denominator on the passing run too (`47 non-DONE GT row(s) checked … 0 with no criteria at all`) and carries two `assertScanned` floors on the checkbox corpus, because BOTH criteria checks read the same `- [ ]` shape and would go quiet together if it ever moved. 17 self-tests including the negative fixture. **It found three real rows on its first run** — GT-435, GT-444, GT-448, all carrying `**Closure:**` prose in the GT-443 shape — and they were given checkable criteria rather than an exemption. | `Governance` | Cross | P2 | XS | `DONE` |

They are baselined BY NAME in `bilingual-suite.mjs` so the class cannot grow; every entry deleted from that list is a translation that actually happened. **CLOSED 2026-07-28 — the baseline is empty and deleted.** Nineteen documents sat in the wrong language slot when the heuristic was switched on. Four were the heuristic's own fault (tables of English ADR titles read as English prose) and were fixed in the library, not excused. | [`GT-627`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-627) | **The committed ADR-conformance corpus was seven rulesets behind its own generator, and six of the seven are security standards.** Found on 2026-07-28 while regenerating for `GT-571`: `generate-adr-rulesets.mjs` wrote 133 rulesets where the repository had 126.
