| [`GT-561`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-561) | **The MCP smoke test times out, failing E2E and cascading into DAST.** The E2E Tests job fails at the MCP stdio/HTTP smoke with `MCP smoke test FAILED: Timed out waiting for initialize (id 1)`; DAST then fails with `MCP Server failed to start after 60s`. The last green run of this pipeline was 2026-06-30. Observed 2026-07-18 in run 29646397424. **Closure (`b408a0ef`):** the server was never broken. The smoke spawned `node dist/main.js mcp serve` from the CLI's dist, and `dc0b9667` (2026-06-30, "decouple mcp-server from smart-cli") DELETED that command -- the exact date of the last green run. The process died instantly with `error: unknown command 'mcp'`, but the stdio path piped stderr and never read it, so an instant hard failure was reported as a 5-second timeout; that misdirection is why it stayed opaque for 19 days. The fix repoints both transports at the standalone gateway, adds a pre-flight check that fails with "build it first" rather than timing out, and captures stderr and the early-exit code into the failure message. No assertion was weakened and NO timeout was raised -- startup measures ~1s, so the 5s limit was never implicated. **Correction to this gap's own premise:** DAST was NOT a downstream cascade from E2E. The jobs are independent and DAST carried its OWN copy of the same stale `mcp serve` invocation, so fixing the smoke alone would have left it red; its start step was repointed too, plus `--allow-no-auth` so ZAP scans a reachable surface. **Three follow-on commits were needed before the criteria could be OBSERVED**, each layer hiding the one beneath: `6cac6cda` -- the SDK 2.0.0 bump had left `package-lock.json` desynced, so `npm ci` refused and eight jobs died on their first step, never reaching the smoke; `b75d43fc` -- the CLI pipeline's `paths` filter did not list `package.json` / `package-lock.json`, so neither the break nor its fix triggered the pipeline they affected (it also listed `release-please-config.json`, deleted in `aed33ba9` -- a filter watching a path that cannot change); `37cbeae1` -- DAST still reported red on `Resource not accessible by integration`, because the ZAP action files its report as a GitHub issue and the workflow grants no `issues: write`; the scan itself was clean (`FAIL-NEW: 0, PASS: 146`), so issue-filing was disabled rather than granting a scanner write access, the report already being uploaded as an artifact. All three criteria are CI-verified in run 29665015800: the MCP smoke passes on both transports (47 tools, 11 resources, 8 prompts, both `tools/call` checks, stdio and HTTP), the E2E Tests job is **success**, and DAST's `Start MCP Server` and `Wait for MCP Server` steps both succeed. **Stated plainly:** the job still shows red for reasons unrelated to this gap -- the CLI's branch-coverage gate (69.3% vs 75, tracked by [`GT-562`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-562)) and 12 pre-existing bilingual-terminology findings in files last touched 2026-07-04 to 07-13. The pipeline is not green. | `MCP Server` | Cross | P1 | M | `DONE` |
| [`GT-562`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-562) | Coverage of the code's decision points sits below the minimum the project requires, and the check blocks **What it means:** Places where the code chooses between two outcomes were never exercised by a test. The project set a minimum, coverage fell under it, and the decision was to earn the difference back rather than lower the bar **Example:** All 969 tests pass and the build still fails: 69 percent of decision points are covered against a required 75 percent. **Closure (`7cffd734`):** the debt was paid rather than written off — branch coverage 70.57% → **75.54%**, jest exits 0, threshold untouched (85 suites, 1250 tests). **The gap's own estimate had the wrong shape:** it predicted ~100-140 tests over interactive wizards, "tests that assert a mock was asked a question." The uncovered branches were not the wizards — they were the **non-interactive, machine-readable paths** (`--format json` across five commands, `init --batch`, the composable engine's report), the paths no human walks by hand, which is exactly why nobody had noticed they were untested; they are also the contract every script and CI job depends on. Three needed a seam before they could be tested at all: `agents --name` did not exist (added; missing agent now returns `RULESET_NOT_FOUND`), `scaffold`'s specs stubbed `process.exit` which does not halt (reading the FIRST envelope makes the assertion mean what it says), and the composable report has no extractable method — but `createComposableEngine()` `require`s its five modes lazily, so they can be mocked by module path, which is why that spec is a separate file. **On the exclusion this gap declared:** it withheld ~100 branches in `adr`/`agents` to avoid blessing the success-envelope defect; that defect was fixed earlier in this campaign, and the new tests now assert the corrected ADR-0073 contract explicitly, pinning the right behaviour instead of freezing the wrong one. | `SDK CLI` | Cross | P2 | M | `DONE` |

Three of the four error categories were NOT defects: 97 of 108 broken links were in a gitignored build-time copy, all 9 mojibake were false positives from a rule lacking a word boundary, and the 5 schema violations were a stale pre-`src/` schema. Two further defects fixed on the way: an unanchored fence matcher that blanked prose instead of code (hiding real emoji violations), and `10-validate-contract-conformance.mjs`, which had crashed on every invocation since the `src/` refactor unnoticed because its job was skipped. Validation now passes for 1535 files and 407 rendered diagrams. The job still fails on a DIFFERENT check, `bilingual-terminology-lint` (12 pre-existing findings). | `Documentation` | Cross | P2 | L | `DONE` |

Six dead path literals compounded it. Fix: `.harness/scripts/lib/paths.mjs` derives the root by marker ascent (`package.json` + `.harness` + `evolith.yaml`) and `resolve()` throws when a path does not exist; 15 scripts migrated and verified identical from three directories. | `Harness` | Cross | P0 | M | `DONE` |

Fix: canonical `Finding` (ADR-0116) with mappers from all six, strictly additive; `FindingOrigin` is a required argument, so an unattributed finding is a compile error. | `Core Domain` | Core | P0 | M | `DONE` |
| [`GT-559`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-559) | **The advisory-authority boundary was prose re-encoded in 60 files, so there was nothing to cite in review and nothing that could detect a violation.** Fix: `evaluateAuthority()` (ADR-0116) answers whether an actor may take an action on an artefact, returning a quotable reason, a stable rule id (AP-R01..R06) and the ADR clause it derives from; the ADR-0097 promotion lifecycle is encoded as data rather than described again. AP-R03 (self-authorization) is ordered ahead of AP-R02 deliberately. | `Core Domain` | Core | P0 | M | `DONE` |
| [`GT-564`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-564) | **The SDK's exported payload types were a fork that did not describe the wire.** REST and MCP call the SAME use case and return the domain object verbatim, so there was no reshaping to justify separate DTOs. The fork declared `passed: boolean` where the API emits `verdict: 'passed'\|'failed'\|'skipped'` -- and `README.md` told users to print `result.data.passed`, which is undefined at runtime; it omitted `gateId`, `rulesetRef` and `rulesetVersion`, which ARE emitted, dropped the REQUIRED `location`, and declared `artifact?`/`remediation?` that no producer emits. `ViolationSeverity` admitted an unreachable `'info'`, forked across two files; `ValidationIssue` had degraded to `severity: string`, losing `MUST\|SHOULD\|COULD`. Fix: `sdk-client` re-exports `@beyondnet/evolith-core-domain` and is bumped to **2.0.0** -- a major bump rather than a deprecation window, because a window preserves types that are actively wrong and any 1.1.0 consumer is already broken at runtime; and a real dependency rather than vendored copies with a parity guard, because two sources of truth plus a watcher is the pattern being removed. 54/54 sdk tests; `tsc` clean for sdk-client, mcp-server and `src/sdk/cli`. Publishing 2.0.0 is deliberately NOT done -- owner's call. | `SDK` | Cross | P1 | L | `DONE` |
| [`GT-565`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-565) | **The SDK's tests mocked `fetch` to return the SDK's OWN invented shape and asserted on it, so its types could drift arbitrarily and stay green** -- the root cause of [`GT-564`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-564). Fix: a contract test asserting the SDK types against the real wire, in two layers, because each is blind to a drift the other catches -- compile-time cannot see a controller change its response without touching the domain type, and runtime cannot see fields absent from the sample or required-vs-optional distinctions. Booting core-api measured ~3s, so the cost argument for compile-time-only did not survive measurement. The runtime checker's key set and required flags are TYPE-DERIVED from the SDK types, so the runtime half cannot drift from the type it enforces; proven to bite with three deliberate breaks, each restored. The suite also ran in NO workflow and is now wired into `ci-cd.yml` (`1875f725`), and its symlink workaround was REMOVED by `e25804dc`, which fixed the underlying ruleset resolution -- the removal is the evidence that fix is real. 43/43. | `Testing` | Cross | P1 | M | `DONE` |
| [`GT-566`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-566) | **Supersedes [`GT-329`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-329), whose closure was unsound: it CLAIMED the topology corpus was unified and left it duplicated.** Its implementing commit `d2b7d2b7` copied the five advanced topologies into `rulesets/topologies/` and deleted nothing -- `--diff-filter=D` is empty -- then closed DONE against the criterion "all topologies under a single canonical location". Ten `RELOCATED.md` tombstones asserted for two weeks that "all tooling, CI scripts, and validators now read from the canonical path" while FOUR CI validators read the directory they called historical, and the two copies quietly diverged. That divergence is what silently disabled ADR-0104 design governance for those same five topologies (`35f79934`): only the `reference/` manifests carried `designProfile`/`phaseProfiles`, and `TopologyCatalogService` de-dupes first-occurrence-wins with `src/` probed first. **Closure (`29d69ae8`, `353cdbd4`):** 180 duplicated files removed; every topology now exists in exactly ONE place. Blast radius was measured by simulating the deletion in a throwaway worktree BEFORE touching anything -- of the ~25 consumers feared, exactly one broke (`17-validate-knowledge-intake`, which read accepted ids from one root and now reads both). 14 doc links, 21 pattern fichas and two E2E specs repointed. Phase A first: the `src/` manifests carried 46 dead references of their own, and a corpus about to become canonical must resolve what it declares. **Explicitly NOT in scope:** collapsing the dual root. The three progressive-axis topologies stay under `reference/` where their full corpora are, and both `src/rulesets/topologies/README.md` and `paths.mjs` document that split as intentional -- deduplication is not the same goal as a single location, and nobody has decided the latter. Historical records in `control-center/` keep the old paths on purpose: rewriting them would erase the evidence that GT-329's closure was unsound. Verified: 8 manifests validate (was 13; five were duplicates), the catalogue resolves all 8 with a designProfile, 11 guards exit 0, 2892 tests green. | `Governance` | Cross | P2 | M | `DONE` |
| [`GT-567`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-567) | **CD apunta al VPS, que esta fuera de alcance: falla en cada push a `main` y nadie lo mira.** `Deploy services (Coolify)` falla desde 2026-07-19 (14+ corridas) porque el host `72.60.63.240` no responde en ningun puerto. NO es una caida de produccion: el entorno objetivo es hoy el cluster kind local `evolith-cluster`, sano y con los 3 servicios `1/1` desde hace 2d14h. El VPS se retoma mas adelante. El defecto real es que CD despliega a un entorno que no se usa, genera un rojo permanente que nadie lee, y `Build & Push` sigue verde publicando imagenes que ningun runtime consume. | `Infra` | Cross | P3 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-568`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-568) | **The Security Audit job fails on a high-severity advisory that lives entirely in dev-only tooling.** `npm audit` reports `brace-expansion` (GHSA-3jxr-9vmj-r5cp, DoS via exponential-time expansion) as high, reached through six paths that are ALL build/lint tooling -- `eslint`, `@eslint/config-array`, `@eslint/eslintrc`, `@typescript-eslint/typescript-estree`, `test-exclude` -- and none of which ship in the published CLI. Observed 2026-07-20 in run 29780735123, and pre-existing: it is not caused by the coverage work in [`GT-562`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-562), which shares that run. The job is a blanket `npm audit` with no production/dev distinction, so a vulnerability that cannot reach a user still reds the pipeline. Two questions to settle together: whether the audit should scope to `--omit=dev` (or record a reasoned exception) so the signal matches the risk, and whether to take the transitive bump at all -- `npm audit fix` rewrites `package-lock.json`, and a full lockfile regeneration is explicitly off-limits here after the ajv override incident. **Closure (`92390d6d`):** auditing before acting inverted the premise. The write-up called it "entirely dev-only tooling," but the same run's tree also carries `js-yaml` (high) and `protobufjs` (moderate), and `npm ls js-yaml --omit=dev` puts js-yaml on the CLI's **production** path — `evolith-cli -> nest-commander -> cosmiconfig -> js-yaml@4.2.0`. So `--omit=dev` would not have gone green; it would have HIDDEN a vulnerability the published CLI ships. Fixed rather than hidden, with targeted `overrides` (not a regeneration): js-yaml → 4.3.0 (+ a scoped `@nestjs/swagger` override so core-api's exact-4.1.1 pin dedupes up), brace-expansion → 1.1.16/2.1.2/5.0.7, protobufjs → 7.6.5. `npm audit --audit-level=high` now reports **0 vulnerabilities**, `npm ci` accepts the lockfile, the diff is 30/-18 lines, build green and all five suites pass (132/168/1174/226/342). The workflow now states its own scope: one root lockfile means it audits the whole monorepo tree (stricter than a CLI-only scan), and a fix-less transitive advisory is patched with an override, not a regeneration. | `Infra` | Cross | P2 | S | `DONE` |

| [`GT-650`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-650) | **The Core holds TWO unreconciled answers to "which artifacts does phase X require", so it cannot publish an artifact catalog at all.** `reference/governance/sdlc/gates/gate-f*.json` names 24 artifacts in human form with `schemaRef`/`producedBy`; `UNIVERSAL_PHASE_ARTIFACTS` in `core-domain` names 18 as slugs across only the three downstream phases. They disagree on names (`CI Pipeline` vs `ci-pipeline-result`), on membership (`source-change-set`, `architecture-drift-result` and `spec-traceability-map` exist only in the constant; `Documentation Delta` and `Acceptance Validation` only in the gates) and — worse — on PHASE: `Coverage Report` is a construction artifact in `gate-f3` and a quality artifact in the constant. Only the first corpus is reachable over HTTP, and it is not the one the evaluator uses. The satellite consequence is measured: `evolith_tracker` `GAP-004` cannot replace its `core-standin` mirror with a `core-sync` source, because there is no single catalog to sync. | `SDLC Governance` | Cross | P1 | M | `DONE` |
| [`GT-651`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-651) | **The marketplace-adapter half of `GT-532`, carved out so its closure does not bury it.** `GT-532` closed on its two acceptance criteria — portfolio views and per-tenant governance packages, both now true — but its TITLE also named marketplace-style adapters, which the criteria never asked for and which did not ship. Registered separately rather than left inside a closed row, because an unstated intent inside a `DONE` row is exactly the staleness this board keeps finding. **Deliberately unscoped:** what an adapter marketplace is for Evolith — a catalogue of provider connectors, a distribution channel for governance packages, or both — is a product decision, and guessing it here would produce acceptance criteria nobody agreed to. **SHAPE CHOSEN 2026-08-03 and the mechanical half DELIVERED 2026-08-08.** The owner's decision — «marketplace» means internal packages published through GitHub Marketplace — names TWO different GitHub surfaces: Marketplace lists Actions and Apps, while GitHub Packages carries npm/NuGet artifacts, and a package cannot be listed on Marketplace. So it splits: the **Action** lands here, the **governance packages** land in `evolith_tracker` as `EAG-23`. The npm libraries are NOT moving — GitHub's own docs require «an access token to publish, install, and delete private, internal, and public packages», so a public install from `npm.pkg.github.com` needs a token where npmjs does not, which would collide with the binding principle that every support Evolith leans on is open and free. **What shipped:** `evolith-validate` was finished work standing in the one directory the channel does not read — «Each repository must contain a single action metadata file at the root», subfolder manifests explicitly «not automatically listed». It was usable cross-repo and permanently unlistable, and nothing here could say so because «publishable» was a property no check had been asked about. `action.yml` moved to the root with `branding`; `64-validate-marketplace-action.mjs` keeps every prerequisite true and was OBSERVED red against the old layout and against the empty tree; `GT-659`'s tenant selection is now reachable from the action, with six ways of naming nothing all reaching the CLI as no flag at all, and the dogfood asserting that an unknown ref BLOCKS with a `SEL-01` naming it. **Still open, and neither is code:** the listing is a human publish step on a release, and the satellite half is `EAG-23`, blocked there on `EAG-01`/`EAG-05`. | `Cross` | Cross | P3 | L | `IN-PROGRESS` |
| [`GT-652`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-652) | **The wire cannot carry five fields the engine reads, and a closure note already called the DTO a "full canonical mirror".** `main.ts` runs the global ValidationPipe with `forbidNonWhitelisted: true`, so a field absent from `EvaluationContextDto` does not arrive stripped -- it 400s the whole evaluation. Guard `60-validate-secret-scan-gate.mjs` runs on every CI run: it extracts the command from the workflow, plants a credential and requires exit 1, and requires a clean tree to exit 0. Matching is forgiving about form (`$id` URL or corpus-relative path) and strict about identity (segment-aligned, so `acl` cannot select `acl-extras`). **A precision the first telling missed:** a selector DOES exist — `--ruleset <id>` resolves through `RULESET_ID_MAP`, a hand-written map of FIFTEEN aliases (`acl`, `adr-0002`, `mcp`…) down a separate `loadRulesetById` path. | [`GT-661`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-661) | **«No selection» means the whole corpus, so a tenant that adopted nothing is still blocked by everything.** The owner's principle is that the Core PROPOSES and the client configures. With no selection the Core has no tenant configuration to consult, refuses to invent one, and labels that scope `core-default` — a PROPOSAL, not a tenant's choice. **The alternative was measured and rejected:** a default that stopped blocking would silently disarm every gate working today, including this repository's own CI. **What changed is that the verdict can be READ:** `ValidationResult.selection` publishes `source` (`caller` or `core-default`), `requested`, `matched`, `unmatched`, `rulesSelected` and `corpusTotal`. The CWEs live in the alerts API and in the SARIF CodeQL uploads. GT-661's `corpusTotal` separates «the Core resolved nothing» from «the caller narrowed to rules that did not run»; a corpus of ZERO is still a hard failure, so GT-474 holds. | [`GT-666`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-666) | **The corpus mapping described 16 conformance controls of three published international standards as Evolith governance invariants — in the one document whose whole purpose is to be checkable by a reader who does not trust us.** `build-iso-5055-mapping.mjs` classified every rule by PATH PREFIX through a `CLASS_BY_FILE` table with `governance` as the fallback, and the table had no `standards/` row. So all 16 rules of the three packs — NIST SP 800-218 (8), ISO/IEC 5055:2021 (4), SLSA v1.0 Build track (4) — matched no prefix, were published as `ruleClass: governance`, and inherited the governance reason verbatim: *«A governance invariant over Evolith artifacts (inheritance, open-core boundary, satellites, evidence). No international structural equivalent.»* **Every clause of that sentence is false of an SSDF practice.** Nothing was stale and nothing was missing — `--check` passed, `46-validate-derived-artifact-order` reported the chain current and at a fixed point, and the artifact stated the opposite of the truth about its own corpus in the register of a considered argument. **DELIVERED 2026-08-09.** The defect was **verified before it was acted on**, not taken from the handover: the 16 rows were read out of the committed `iso-5055-mapping.json` and the note quoted above is its literal text. **The fix derives, it does not enumerate.** A `['standards/', 'international-standard']` row would have made the symptom disappear and left the mechanism intact — the fourth pack, landing anywhere else, would fall through to `governance` again. Classification now reads **the pack's own top-level `standard` block**, a declaration that travels with the file, so a pack classifies correctly wherever it lands; the two shipped shapes (`{id}` for ISO/IEC 5055, `{name, edition}` for SSDF and SLSA) are both read, because a reader of one shape would have silently dropped two of three packs and still reported a pass. **The directory is the second signal and it is enforced in the OTHER direction:** a `*.rules.json` under `standards/` carrying rules without a `standard` declaration now **fails the generator**, so the one remaining way back in is a build failure rather than a silent default. **Each note is derived from that same declaration and names the standard the rule belongs to** — a class-wide sentence would have been the same defect one level down, one text asserted over every standard and true of none in particular; the ISO/IEC 5055 pack gets a different note from the other two, and which one applies is itself derived by comparing the pack's declared standard against the one the weakness index publishes. **`65-validate-standards-rule-class.mjs` + 19-case self-test**, wired into `Governance guards (GT-578)`, holds both directions and **was observed failing against the REAL pre-fix artifact read out of git** — 64 findings, 16 rules x 4 rules broken — rather than against a reconstructed fixture that would only ever agree with what the author believed was wrong. **The guard's own self-test found a defect in the guard:** counting only DECLARING packs as the denominator meant a tree whose packs had all lost their declaration died with «the corpus moved», the one diagnosis certainly wrong — red either way, naming the wrong cause. **Measured after: 16 rows `international-standard`, `governance` 71 → 55, corpus totals unmoved at 412 rules / 180 files, and every pinned count in `rule-corpus-triage.spec.ts` unchanged** — this reclassifies the mapping, it does not touch the corpus. **NOT claimed, and found while measuring:** the four `ISO5055-*` rows still carry `analyser.adoptable: no`, whose stated meaning is «must be authored», while the pack is decided by an off-the-shelf analyser's SARIF (`GT-662`…`GT-664`). That is a second false field in the same rows, it is derivable from `enforce.config.analyser`, and it is registered separately rather than smuggled into this diff. | `Evolith Core` | Cross | P1 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-667`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-667) | **The corpus mapping told an auditor that four rules decided by an off-the-shelf analyser were rules somebody still had to write — and counted them into the handler backlog that whole time.** `analyser.adoptable` has one stated meaning in `build-iso-5055-mapping.mjs`'s own header: `no` means *«the predicate is repository- or product-specific and must be authored»*. The four `ISO5055-*` rows carried `no` while `GT-662`…`GT-664` had already shipped that pack as an **adapter over a free analyser's SARIF** — the predicate is decided by ESLint or semgrep or a tenant's own SARIF log, and nothing about it was ever going to be authored. `analyser.examples` was `[]` on all four, so the row asserted a verdict and named nothing a reader could check. **DELIVERED 2026-08-09.** The defect was **read out of the committed artifact before anything was touched**, and it is not new information: `GT-666` found it while measuring, wrote it into its own closure record as NOT claimed, and left it precisely because it moves the arithmetic `GT-598` exists to produce. **The fix derives, following the pattern `GT-666` established for `ruleClass`:** the rules declare the analyser themselves in `enforce.config.analyser`, and adoptability is read from that declaration instead of asserted about it from a table in the generator. **The signal was chosen against the corpus, not by inspection of the four rows it had to move:** a walk of all 412 rules found `enforce` on 10 and `enforce.config.analyser` on exactly 4, and the other 6 are the counterexample that settles which field is right — they name `dependency-cruiser` and then supply the predicate themselves (`HXA-07` declares the tool and then writes its own `from`/`to` patterns over this repository's own `src/domain` and `src/core` test files — the literal regex is in the catalog entry), where the tool is the ENGINE and the rule is Evolith's, written against this repository's own layout, which is «repository-specific» verbatim. **Deriving from `enforce.tool` would have flipped `HXA-06` and `HXA-07` to `yes` on the strength of a third-party binary appearing in the clause**, which is not the claim `adoptable` makes. **`yes` and not `partial`, argued rather than assumed:** the shipped ESLint path reaches only 11 of the 138 weaknesses, but that is COVERAGE, and `partial` means *the rest is ours to author* — here the rest is closed by a tenant pointing the same adapter at a better analyser, so the handler work is zero and coverage stays reported where it belongs (`notEvaluableHere` and the `GT-569` advisory). **A conflicting `MAP` claim on a declaring rule now fails the build**, because one of the two would be wrong and neither should win quietly. **Measured after, and every figure moved on purpose:** `summary.analyserAdoptable` 42 → 46, `analyserAdoptableFraction` 0.1019 → 0.1117, including-partial 0.1578 → 0.1675, `byClass.international-standard.adoptable` 0 → 4; `handlerBacklog.adoptableFromAnalyser` 5 → 9, including-partial 10 → 14, `adoptedFractionOfBacklog` 0.0962 → 0.1731, including-partial 0.1923 → 0.2692, and **`remainderToAuthor` 42 → 38 — the backlog had been overstated by four**. `realBacklogSize` 52, corpus 412 rules / 180 files, `mappedToIso5055` 37 and `adoptedFraction` unchanged; a row-by-row diff of the regenerated artifact against the committed one shows **exactly 4 rows changed and zero non-`analyser` fields changed anywhere**. **The share moved because the description was corrected, not because anything was adopted**, and both READMEs say so where the jump appears — reading it as progress would be reading a fixed measurement as a result. **The eight SSDF and four SLSA rules stay at `no`, and that is a verdict rather than an omission:** they carry no `enforce` block because `SsdfRuleHandler`/`SlsaRuleHandler` decide them natively, and no off-the-shelf analyser answers «does this repository satisfy SSDF PW.4.1» — those predicates read this repository's own workflows, manifests and evidence, so the work genuinely had to be authored and was (`GT-659`, `GT-665`). One pack declares an analyser, the other two ship a handler, and the difference is visible in the corpus rather than argued in prose. | `Evolith Core` | Cross | P1 | S | `DONE` |
| [`GT-668`](./gap-reference-catalog.md#gt-668) | **`GT-666`'s proof that its guard had ever been observed failing was anchored to a MOVING REF, so it stopped being evidence at the exact moment the fix landed.** The one case that reads the real pre-fix artifact obtained it with `git show ${PRE_FIX_REF}:iso-5055-mapping.json`, `PRE_FIX_REF` defaulting to `origin/develop`. That is true only while the fix lives on a branch: **`59d62bae` merged, `origin/develop` began serving the CORRECTED artifact, and the case asserting 64 findings found 0** — green on its own branch, red the moment it mattered, blocking the `develop` → `main` promotion PR **#483** in `Governance guards (GT-578)`. **Reproduced before acting**, not taken from the handover: `node --test` on `develop` at `59d62bae` fails that one case with `0 !== 64` while the other 18 pass. **The worse half is the one nobody would have seen:** the case carried `if (before.status !== 0) return void assert.ok(true, 'SKIPPED: …')` for shallow clones, so in a checkout with a truncated history the same rot would have **passed in silence** rather than failing — an escape hatch out of the only case that proves the guard was ever red. **DELIVERED 2026-08-09.** The comment's reasoning was right and is kept: *«read out of git rather than reconstructed … Reconstructed fixtures agree with whatever the author believed was wrong; this one cannot.»* That argues for a REAL pre-fix artifact, not for reading a branch at test time — so the artifact is **frozen in the repository**: `.harness/fixtures/standards-rule-class/iso-5055-mapping.pre-gt-666.json`, taken from `01308346` (`59d62bae^`, blob `6684e8a4`), **not trimmed**, and verified byte-identical to that blob once its one added `_fixture` provenance key is dropped. The `git show`, the `PRE_FIX_REF` env var and the shallow-clone skip are **deleted** — a skip can only ever hide a failure. **Assertions unchanged and unweakened: 64 findings, 16 of them `is classified &#96;governance&#96;`**, measured against the frozen fixture and today's packs. One case is ADDED rather than any relaxed: a fixture «refreshed» from the live mapping is RED, and that was **observed** — the refresh was simulated and turns 2 of 20 cases red, so the negative fixture cannot silently become a copy of the artifact it exists to catch. Meta-guards re-run: `42` 78/78 classified, `43` 54/54 observed failing. | `Evolith Core` | Cross | P1 | XS | `DONE` |

**Wave 2026-06-28 (GT-380 / L1c — phase-gates wired + OPA toolchain unbroken):** `phase-gates.rego` now exports `violations` and is aggregated by `main.rego` (+ input schema + 5 rego tests), fixing the audit's triple-disconnect. **Root-caused & fixed a silently-failing CI gate:** a macOS-arm64 `opa` binary was committed at `.harness/bin/opa`, so the linux `opa-parity` runner hit "Exec format error" on every run — untracked it (`.harness/bin/` was already gitignored) and hardened `compile-opa-wasm.mjs` to re-download if the cached binary doesn't run on the platform. **Validated locally (arm64-darwin):** `opa test` **202/202**, `build:policy` compiles; **and the dispatched `opa-parity` CI run is now GREEN** (run 28348928891). `GT-380` → `IN-PROGRESS` (phase-gates + toolchain done; canonical `input.context` single-builder + re-anchor `dod`/`compliance` remain). Commits `68414dcc`, plus the phase-gates wiring commit. **Follow-on:** `dod.rego` re-anchored off the `input.story` root to `input.context.dod` (legacy `input.story` fallback retained, 3 new tests, `opa test` 205/205, opa-parity CI green run 28349180408) — commit `8a249693`. `compliance-baseline.rego` likewise re-anchored to `input.context.{spec,satellite}` (legacy fallback retained, 2 new tests, `opa test` 207/207, opa-parity CI green run 28349308125) — commit `f0d1cee7`. **Threading landed:** a pure `evaluationFactsFromContext` projector rides an optional `EvaluationFacts` on the manifest (`SatelliteManifest.facts` → `WorkspaceEvaluationContext.facts` → `OpaInputBuilder` merges `input.context`/`input.gate`/`input.evidence` additively, byte-for-byte fallback) — REST/CLI/MCP adapters unchanged (commit `2dc2a328`; core-domain 670/670). **Story-decoupling (AC1) landed:** `dod.rego` `input.story` fallback removed (reads only `input.context.dod`); `Functional`/`Technical Stories` removed from canonical gates `gate-f2/f3.json` + legacy `phase-gates` rulesets + `evidence-validator`/`blocking-criteria-validator`/MCP `sdlc.tools`; story schemas marked deprecated (commit `f0016c2e`; `opa test` 204/204, CLI 1092/1092, MCP 171/171). **`GT-380` → `DONE`** (AC1 ✓, AC2 parity 0-drift + OPA green ✓). **Adversarial review surfaced a real defect → filed as `GT-382`:** the threaded facts are currently INERT because `OpaEvaluator` filters violations by `v.id === rule.id` (path-derived `opa-dod`) while policies emit `DOD-*`/`CB-*`/`PG-*` — the verdict-consumption fix has FS-path blast radius (needs a no-facts→no-opinion guard) so it is split out, not bolted on here.

**Wave 2026-06-29 (GT-382 — threaded facts now ENFORCE):** `OpaEvaluator` now prefix-matches the 3 context-aware rule ids (`opa-dod`→`DOD-`, `opa-compliance-baseline`→`CB-`, `opa-phase-gates`→`PG-`) so a threaded fact's violation flips the gate verdict; collision-free (only those 3 policies emit those prefixes — ~190 ids enumerated). FS-path no-regression secured by "no-facts→no-opinion" guards: `dod_declared`/`spec_declared` on all `dod.rego`/`compliance-baseline.rego` rules (phase-gates already no-ops without `input.gate`); `compliance-baseline` also drops the dead `input.spec` fallback. Tests: 5 OpaEvaluator unit cases + dod/compliance no-facts rego cases + 2 pipeline cases. **Adversarially reviewed** (2 refutation lenses + synthesis → MERGE, no blockers; FS-path no-regression confirmed by live `opa eval`). Verified: core-domain **677/677**, `opa test` **206/206**, build:policy compiles, CLI 1092/1092, MCP 171/171, **opa-parity CI green** (run 28352059534). Commit `175de3da`. **`GT-382` → `DONE`.** Non-blocking follow-up (chip): `compile-opa-wasm.mjs` installs `policy.wasm` only to `sdk/cli/rulesets/opa/` while `findCoreFromSatellite` resolves `corePath` to the repo root (no wasm there → evaluator fails closed) — wasm install-path vs lookup-path co-location.

**Wave 2026-06-29 (GT-376–379 reconciliation — per-AC verification against code):** ran a 4-agent verification of the R0–R3 acceptance criteria. **`GT-378` → `DONE`** (both ACs MET, verified: `/v1/evaluate` accepts `EvaluationContextDto`→`EvaluationResult` via `EnvelopeInterceptor`; `mapPipelineVerdict` adapter; opa-parity 0-drift; SDK/CLI/Tracker unbroken). The other three stay `IN-PROGRESS` with precise remaining blockers (chips filed): **`GT-376`** — only AC-1 open: ADR-0101 is `Proposed`, not board-`Accepted` (the other 3 ACs MET — ADR-0100 D1 superseded, UP-002/redesign deliverables SUPERSEDED, no live `IProductRepository`/`POST /products`, `CoreGateVerdict`/`WAIVE` landed). **`GT-377`** — AC-2 MET; AC-1 open (`rulesets/schema/evaluation-context.schema.json`/`evaluation-result.schema.json` do not exist — only the TS contracts + `SCHEMA_VERSION` constants); AC-3 open (the `.eslintrc.js:78-80` boundary guard is broken under ESLint 9.39.4 AND not invoked by any workflow — `grep lint:boundaries .github/workflows` = 0). **`GT-379`** — AC-1 now MET (added an explicit checkpoint non-mutation test: non-binding, idempotent, read-only agent proposal — `propose-phase-advance.use-case.spec.ts`); AC-2 MET; AC-3 open (blueprint/deployment kind-evaluators not implemented — contracts/enums exist, evaluators don't; deferred as speculative → chip).

**Wave 2026-06-29 (ADR-0101 Accepted → `GT-376` DONE):** the Architecture Board **accepted ADR-0101** — Evolith Core is a stateless evaluation engine (`EvaluationContext` → `EvaluationResult`; product/tenant/initiative are opaque context, never Core entities), superseding ADR-0100 Decision 1. Status flipped to `Accepted (2026-06-29)` on ADR-0101 (EN/ES) and ADR-0100 (EN/ES — Decisions 2–6 + diagnosis retained, Decision 1 superseded). That closes GT-376's last open AC → **`GT-376` → `DONE`** (all 3 ACs ✓). Umbrella `GT-375` AC "ADR-0101 accepted" now ✓ (GT-375 stays IN-PROGRESS pending its last child, `GT-381`). Remaining épico open items: `GT-377` (schemas + ESLint-9 guard — chips), `GT-379` (blueprint/deployment evaluators — chip), `GT-381` (R5 docs/Tracker integration), `GT-382` follow-up (wasm path — chip).

**Wave 2026-06-29 (GT-377 AC-1 — Contract Schema Registry):** added `rulesets/schema/evaluation-context.schema.json` + `evaluation-result.schema.json` (draft-07, faithful to the TS contracts) and a round-trip jest test (`contracts.schema.spec.ts`, 6/6) — validates the canonical context/result and rejects: context missing `kinds`, result missing mandatory `schemaVersion`, `DecisionRecommendation.binding` ≠ literal `false`. **GT-377 AC-1 + AC-2 ✓**; `GT-377` stays IN-PROGRESS on **AC-3 only** (ESLint-9 boundary guard + CI wiring — chip).

**Wave 2026-06-29 (GT-379 AC-3 — blueprint + deployment evaluators → `GT-379` DONE):** implemented `createBlueprintKindEvaluator` and `createDeploymentKindEvaluator` (kind-evaluators.ts) + wired both into the `EvaluationOrchestrator` (app.module.ts). Stateless, side-effect-free, at the same altitude as the topology/checkpoint evaluators: blueprint resolves the declared `ctx.blueprintRef` against the Core blueprint definitions on disk (does NOT use the mutating `ValidateBlueprintUseCase`); deployment evaluates the declared `ctx.deployment` (environment/releaseRef/status). Both SKIP when their context is absent → no FS-path effect. 7 new unit tests (kind-evaluators.spec.ts 17/17; core-api 129/129 runnable). With AC-1 (checkpoint non-mutation test) + AC-2 (binding false) already ✓, **`GT-379` → `DONE`** — every requested kind (architecture/blueprint/topology/checkpoint/deployment) now evaluates through the contract.

**Wave 2026-06-29 (GT-382 wasm-path follow-up — OPA enforcement now runs at runtime):** `compile-opa-wasm.mjs` now installs `policy.wasm` to BOTH runtime lookup paths (`<root>/rulesets/opa/policy.wasm` co-located with the .rego sources that `findCoreFromSatellite` resolves to, AND `sdk/cli/rulesets/opa/`), so the `OpaEvaluator` finds the wasm instead of fail-closing. Both paths gitignored (build artifact, never committed); CI's core-domain job now runs `build:policy` before tests. New `satellite-evaluation-pipeline.integration.spec.ts` drives the REAL wasm end-to-end (2/2 with wasm present; self-skips cleanly when absent). core-domain **686/686**. Integrated from the spun-off worktree `claude/cranky-margulis-44d8a8` (commit `04bab3de`), re-applied onto main to preserve the `downloadOpa` hardening + the authoritative board. **GT-382 wasm-path follow-up resolved.** (Superseded/declined parallel branches: `claude/hardcore-engelbart` = duplicate GT-382 logic already on main; `claude/amazing-chaplygin` = GT-379 done differently on main.)

**Wave 2026-06-29 (GT-381 R5 — artifact-ownership reclassification, partial):** added a normative **"Artifact Ownership — Core definitions vs. external work items (ADR-0101 / GT-380)"** section to `sdlc-evolith-artifact-mapping.md` (EN+ES). Classifies agile work items (Epics/Functional/Technical/User Stories/Agile Backlog/Story Seeds/Epic Candidate Matrix/Sprints/tasks) as **`ExternalReferenceContext`** owned by the Tracker/external tools — never Core gate artifacts — and reconciles the doc with the accepted ADR-0101 + the GT-380 gate changes (no Core gate mandates a story; templates remain Core authoring guidance, instances are Tracker-owned). Resolves GT-381 AC-1 "zero divergent formats" at the framing level. `GT-381` stays **IN-PROGRESS**: remaining = mechanical reclassification of the per-phase tables, "Core degrades without the Tracker" + CLI/MCP/API parity (BR-008), and the actual **Tracker integration** (needs the external Tracker component). PR #147 (`vigorous-gauss`, GT-377 AC-3 ESLint guard) is OPEN but CONFLICTING vs develop — to be merged once that session rebases + goes green.

**Wave 2026-06-30 (GT-384 R1 — real Core-evaluation adapters, implemented):** shipped the two production `ICoreEvaluationPort` adapters in `@evolith/agent-runtime`, replacing the simulated default. `InProcessCoreEvaluationAdapter` is a thin seam over the Core's `EvaluationOrchestrator.evaluate(ctx)` (structural type — no concrete import; the host injects a constructed orchestrator). `HttpCoreEvaluationAdapter` calls the Core API `POST /api/v1/evaluate` and unwraps the ADR-0073 `{success,data}` envelope (tolerates a raw body; throws on non-2xx). Wired into the service composition root (`apps/agent-runtime-api/.../runtime.factory.ts`): `AGENT_RUNTIME_CORE_ENDPOINT` (+ optional `AGENT_RUNTIME_CORE_TOKEN`) graduates the port to the real Core; the deterministic stub stays the offline/test default (design rule #5). New parity spec proves stub↔real 0 contract drift and that the real path populates `rulesExecuted`/`policiesApplied` (the stub leaves them empty — the exact limitation this closes). Verified: agent-runtime `tsc -b` green, jest **22/22** (16→22, +6), agent-runtime-api build green. `GT-384` → **IN-PROGRESS** (adapters + REST wiring done; remaining = construct the real in-process orchestrator in a host + a live end-to-end test against a running Core). `GT-383` umbrella → **IN-PROGRESS**. Commit pending push to `develop`.

**Wave 2026-06-30 (GT-386 R3 — filesystem-durable scheduler + memory, implemented):** added `FileSchedulerAdapter` (`ISchedulerPort`) and `FileMemoryAdapter` (`IMemoryPort`) to `@evolith/agent-runtime`, each backed by a JSON file so the scheduler backlog and working memory survive a process restart — the defining gap of the in-memory defaults. Chose the zero-infra filesystem backend (single-node / mounted volume); a networked queue/cron or Redis/vector store is a sibling adapter behind the same port. Durable memory env-wired into `runtime.factory.ts` via `AGENT_RUNTIME_STATE_DIR` (the scheduler is not part of the runtime bundle — a host scheduling loop drives it); in-memory stays the offline/test default. Spec proves durability across a fresh instance on the same file (= restart). Verified: agent-runtime `tsc -b` green, jest **28/28** (22→28, +6), agent-runtime-api build green. `GT-386` → **IN-PROGRESS** (fs-durable done; networked cron/queue + true cron-expression scheduling remain a future swap).

**Wave 2026-06-30 (GT-388 R5 — public-contract freeze, partial):** froze the public API surface of `@evolith/agent-runtime` short of the version bump. New `public-surface.spec.ts` guard pins the runtime value surface of the `.` and `./adapters` entrypoints (exact frozen set of 23 adapter/factory exports) so any add/remove/rename is a deliberate, reviewed change; `./ports` is a type-only surface frozen by consumers' `tsc`. Added a normative **"Versioning & contract stability"** section to the package README (EN+ES): SemVer policy, `schemaVersion` evolves independently (incompatible-only), deprecation = one-minor `@deprecated` before a major, plus the pre-1.0 note. Verified: jest **30/30** (28→30; the frozen list matched the live surface exactly). Version intentionally NOT bumped — stays `0.1.0`. `GT-388` → **IN-PROGRESS** (freeze + policy done; the `0.1.0`→`1.0.0` bump remains, gated on closing `GT-384`).

The architecture doc keeps only its forward-looking sections.

**Wave 2026-07-03 (UltraCode harness productization audit):** Re-ran the Winston/UltraCode audit path after cleanup. Coverage evidence stayed strong (`run-evolith-deep.mjs --markdown` = 9/9 solid; `run-evolith-topology.mjs --markdown` = 168/168 across all 8 accepted topologies), but real Agent Runtime productization surfaced 6 new open gaps: `GT-413` (real OPA adapter cannot evaluate because schemas are loaded as OPA data), `GT-414` (policyRef namespace drift), `GT-415` (public-surface/SemVer drift; agent-runtime tests red), `GT-416` (`.harness` manifest exposes only 7 governed capabilities out of 110 executable script/playbook assets), `GT-417` (semantic tracking fails because several `DONE` gaps lack closure registry records and checked criteria), and `GT-418` (self-improving loop seeded but not enforced in CI/Agent Runtime). Both rule engines were checked: Native/topology audits pass, while the runtime OPA CLI path fails closed until `GT-413`/`GT-414` are fixed.

**Wave 2026-07-26 (enforcement landed; GT-577 / GT-574):** **Enforcement landed, and GT-577 closed on CI evidence rather than on a green tick.** `GT-577` → DONE: the dogfood workflow ran on a real runner (run 30228607519, `develop`) with both jobs green, and the closure rests on what the log asserts, not on the colour — `OK: 34 blocking violation(s) of 274 issue(s), agreeing with the report.` plus the negative half `OK: the gate blocked as expected`. The composite action is now dogfooded and under regression, which was the larger half of that gap. `GT-574`'s closure record was re-verified against the GitHub API and enriched with the observed values: **`enforce_admins` is now TRUE on both `main` and `develop`**, required contexts went 6 → 7, and **`CodeQL SAST` is required**, so the audit finding "0 of the 6 required checks is a security check" no longer holds; `develop`, which previously returned 404 *Branch not protected* while being the branch every change lands on, now carries the same seven. This is the first time an *Enforced* rung in this corpus is backed by configuration rather than by prose. Registered separately in this window: `GT-596`…`GT-600` (international technical-debt artifacts — ISO/IEC 33020:2019, 5055:2021, 25040:2024, OMG ATDM V2, OpenSSF Scorecard/SLSA/SSDF) and `GT-601`…`GT-608` (component assessment). **Unmoved and now the critical path:** `GT-570` — npm still serves 1.1.0 of 2026-07-18 on all three packages checked, so the published artifact still predates the security wave the public CHANGELOG enumerates. No `src/` change landed in this window; the code-level gaps are unchanged by construction, and their fixes were re-verified as still holding (denominator 111 checked / 269 skipped / 380 total, a 123 KB envelope surviving a pipe, the `evolith` bin alias, MCP stdio invocable). Counters recomputed from the rows across both status vocabularies.

**Ordering:** one table, ordered by status (pending then completed), then criticality (`P0` → `P1` → `P2` → `P3`), then complexity (`XS` → `S` → `M` → `L` → `XL`). `GT-*` IDs link to the [Gap Reference Catalog](./gap-reference-catalog.md); `MT-A*` IDs link to the supporting [Multi-Topology implementation plan](../audits/multi-topology-reference-corpus-implementation-plan.md).
